1214 
1215 
1216 The emphasis on a crossing occurring ``on the $E_{n+1}$-page" in Definition~\ref{def:classicalcrossdiff} may seem counter-intuitive. However, this is clarified in Proposition~\ref{prop:cross-dr-En} and Example~\ref{exam:classcrossdiff} that follow.
1217 
1218 \begin{proposition}\label{prop:cross-dr-En}
1219     The Adams differential $d_r(x)=y$ has a crossing on the $E_{n+1}$-page if and only if the corresponding $\delta_n$-extension
1220     $$d^{\delta_n}_r(x)=\lambda^{r-n-1}y$$
1221     for
1222     $$\delta_{n}:\nu X/\lambda^{n}\to \Sus^{1,-n}\nu X$$
1223     has a crossing.
1224 \end{proposition}
1225 \begin{proof}
1226     By Propositions \ref{prop:30e8b746}, \ref{prop:59f111f} and \ref{prop:6de7d130}, a crossing of $d^{\delta_n}_r(x)=\lambda^{r-n-1}y$ takes the form
1227     $$d^{\delta_n}_{r-a-b}(\lambda^a x')=\lambda^{r-n-1-b}y',$$
1228     where $0<a\le n-1$, $0\le b\le r-n-1$, 
1229     $$\lambda^a x'\in E_\infty^{s+a,t+a,t}(\nu X/\lambda^n)\iso Z_{n-a}^{s+a,t+a}(X)/B_{1+a}^{s+a,t+a}(X),$$
1230     and
1231     $$y'\in E_\infty^{s+r-b, t+r-b-1}(\nu X).$$
1232     By Corollary \ref{cor:2a636737}, we see that this crossing corresponds to the classical Adams differential
1233     $$d_{r-a-b}(x')=y'.$$
1234 \end{proof}
1235 
1236 \begin{remark} \label{nocrossE2}
1237     From Definition~\ref{def:classicalcrossdiff}, it immediately follows that there are no crossings of any differential on the $E_2$-page, as this would require $0< a \le n-1 = 0$. According to Proposition~\ref{prop:cross-dr-En}, this reflects the fact that $\delta_1$-extensions have no crossings for degree reasons.
1238 \end{remark}
1239 
1240 \begin{example} \label{exam:classcrossdiff}
1241     We examine the following classical Adams differentials from stem 38 to stem 37:
1242     $$d_3(e_1) = h_1 t, \ d_4(h_0h_3h_5) = h_0^2 x,$$
1243     where both targets, $h_1 t$ and $h_0^2 x$, reside within the same $(s,t)$-bidegree in $\Ext_A^{7,44}$.
1244 
1245     According to Definition~\ref{def:classicalcrossdiff}, this nonzero $d_3$-differential is a crossing of the $d_4$-differential on both the $E_3$-page and the $E_4$-page, but not on the $E_2$-page or any $E_r$-page for $r \ge 5$.
1246 
1247     This corresponds to the following facts: 
1248     \begin{enumerate}
1249         \item For the map $\delta_2: S^{0,0}/\lambda^2 \rightarrow S^{1,-2}$, the differential
1250         $$d_3^{\delta_2} (\lambda e_1) = \lambda h_1 t$$
1251         is a crossing for the differential
1252         $$d_4^{\delta_2} (h_0h_3h_5) = \lambda h_0^2 x.$$
1253 
1254         \item For the map $\delta_3: S^{0,0}/\lambda^3 \rightarrow S^{1,-3}$, the differential
1255         $$d_3^{\delta_3} (\lambda e_1) = h_1 t$$
1256         is a crossing for the differential
1257         $$d_4^{\delta_3} (h_0h_3h_5) = h_0^2 x.$$
1258         In both cases (1) and (2), the target elements have the same weight. 
1259 
1260         \item In comparison, for the map $\delta_1: S^{0,0}/\lambda \rightarrow S^{1,-1}$, we have the differential
1261         $$d_4^{\delta_1} (h_0h_3h_5) = \lambda^2 h_0^2 x,$$
1262         which does not have any crossing differential. The only differential whose target resides in the same $(s,t)$-bidegree is 
1263         $$d_3^{\delta_1} (e_1) = \lambda h_1 t,$$
1264         but its target has a different weight. 
1265 
1266         \item For later pages, specifically for $r \ge 4$, and considering the synthetic spectrum $S^{0,0}/\lambda^r$, there is still a crossing for the differential
1267         $$d_4^{\delta_r} (\lambda^{r-3} h_0h_3h_5) = h_0^2 x.$$
1268         However, the element $h_0h_3h_5$ itself does not survive to the synthetic Adams $E_\infty$-page, as it supports a nonzero Adams differential:
1269         $$d_4 (h_0h_3h_5) = \lambda^3 h_0^2 x.$$
1270     \end{enumerate}
1271 \end{example}
1272 
1273 \section{Extensions on a classical $E_r$-page} \label{sec:extenpage}
1274 
1275 To state the Generalized Leibniz Rule in terms of the classical Adams spectral sequence, we need to define $f$-extensions not only on homotopy groups but \emph{on the $E_r$-page} as well.
1276 
1277 \begin{notation}
1278 For a map between classical spectra $f: X\to Y$, consider the associated synthetic map from Notation~\ref{not:fhat}
1279 $$\hat f: \Sus^{0,e(f)}\nu X\to \nu Y.$$
1280 For any $2\le r\le \infty$, we denote the following mod $\lambda^{r-1}$ reduction maps by $\hat f_{r-1}$:
1281 $$\hat f_{r-1}: \Sus^{0,e(f)}\nu X/\lambda^{r-1}\to \nu Y/\lambda^{r-1}.$$
1282 \end{notation}
1283 
1284 The $E_0$-page of the $\hat f_{r-1}$-ESS is isomorphic to
1285 $$\begin{aligned}
1286     \ESS{\hat f_{r-1}}_0^{s,t,t-k+e(f)}\iso & E_\infty^{s,t,t-k}(\nu X/\lambda^{r-1})\oplus E_\infty^{s,t,t-k+e(f)}(\nu Y/\lambda^{r-1})\\
1287     \iso & \big(Z_{r-1-k}^{s,t}(X)/B_{1+k}^{s,t}(X)\big)\oplus \big(Z_{r-1-k+e(f)}^{s,t}(Y)/B_{1+k-e(f)}^{s,t}(Y)\big).
1288 \end{aligned}$$
1289 A nontrivial $d^{\hat f_{r-1}}_n$ differential can be interpreted as a map from the subgroup
1290 \begin{equation}\label{eq:eae450fe}
1291   \ZESS{\hat f_{r-1}}_{n-1}^{s,t,t-k}(X) \subset  Z_{r-1-k}^{s,t}(X)/B_{1+k}^{s,t}(X)
1292 \end{equation}
1293 to the quotient group 
1294 \begin{equation}\label{eq:334248d2}
1295     \big(Z_{r-1-k-n+e(f)}^{s+n,t+n}(Y)/B_{1+k+n-e(f)}^{s+n,t+n}(Y) \big)/\BESS{\hat f_{r-1}}_{n-1}^{s+n,t+n,t-k}(Y).
1296 \end{equation}
1297 The differential $d^{\hat f_{r-1}}_n$ is trivial for degree reasons when 
1298 $$n<\AF(f)\text{ or }n>r-2-k+e(f).$$
1299 
1300     
1301 \begin{definition}\label{def:6c076a33}
1302     Let $x\in Z^{s,t}_{r-1}(X)$ and $y\in Z^{s+n,t+n}_{r-1-n+e(f)}(Y)$ for some 
1303     $$e(f)\le n\le r-2+e(f).$$
1304     We say that there is an $(f,E_r)$-extension from $x$ to $y$, denoted by
1305     \begin{equation}\label{eq:b98be76a}
1306     d_{n}^{f,E_r}(x)=y
1307     \end{equation}
1308     if there exists a synthetic $\hat f_{r-1}$-extension
1309     \begin{equation}\label{eq:a020bad3}
1310         d_{n}^{\hat f_{r-1}}(x)=\lambda^{n-e(f)} y.
1311     \end{equation}
1312     where $x$ is viewed as an element of the subgroup (\ref{eq:eae450fe}) with $k=0$, and $\lambda^{n-e(f)} y$ is viewed as an element of the quotient group (\ref{eq:334248d2}) with $k=0$. 
1313 
1314    We say that this $(f,E_r)$-extension in (\ref{eq:b98be76a}) is \emph{essential} if the corresponding synthetic $\hat f_{r-1}$-extension in (\ref{eq:a020bad3}) is an essential differential in the $\hat f_{r-1}$-ESS.
1315     
1316     For $r=\infty$, we similar define an $(f,E_\infty)$-extension using the corresponding synthetic $\hat f$-extension.
1317 \end{definition}
1318 
1319 
1320

1385 
1386 \begin{remark} \label{def:fErextess}
1387 Consider an $(f,E_r)$-extension $d_{n}^{f,E_r}(x)=y$ in (\ref{eq:b98be76a}).
1388     The element $y$ should be interpreted as a coset with indeterminacy given by $B_{1+n-e(f)}^{s+n,t+n}(Y)$, plus the sum of images of $d^{\hat f_{r-1}}_{<n}$ differentials.   This $(f,E_r)$-extension is \emph{essential} if this coset does not contain 0.
1389 \end{remark}
1390 
1391 \begin{example} \label{exam:extonEn}
1392 We present the following examples of $(f, E_r)$-extensions.
1393     \begin{enumerate}
1394         \item Consider $f = \nu: S^3 \rightarrow S^0$. We have an $(f, E_2)$-extension:
1395         $$d_1^{f, E_2} (h_5) = h_2 h_5,$$
1396         where $h_5 \in \Ext_A^{1,32}$ and $h_2 h_5 \in \Ext_A^{2,36}$.
1397         
1398         In fact, the classical homotopy class $\nu \in \pi_3$ is detected by $h_2$ with $\AF=1$ in Ext, and this $(f, E_2)$-extension simply states that, in Ext, the product of $h_5$ by $h_2$ is $h_2 h_5$.
1399 
1400         \item For the same map $f$ as in $(1)$, we have an $(f, E_3)$-extension:
1401         $$d_2^{f, E_3} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p,$$
1402         where $h_0 h_4^2 \in \Ext_A^{3,33}$ and $h_0 p \in \Ext_A^{5,38}$.
1403         
1404         By definition, $\hat{f} = [h_2]$, detected by $h_2$ in tridegree $(s,t,w) = (3,4,4)$ of the synthetic Adams $E_2$-page. This $(f, E_3)$-extension states that for the synthetic map 
1405         $$\hat{f_{2}} = [h_2]/\lambda^2: S^{3,4}/\lambda^2 \rightarrow S^{0,0}/\lambda^2,$$ 
1406         there exists a synthetic $\hat{f_{2}}$-extension
1407         $$d_2^{\hat{f_{2}}} (h_0h_4^2) = \lambda h_0 p.$$
1408         This is equivalent to the existence of a synthetic homotopy class $[h_0 h_4^2]$ in $\pi_{30+3, 33+4} S^{3,4}/\lambda^2$, detected by $h_0h_4^2$ on the synthetic Adams $E_\infty$-page, that maps to the unique class $[\lambda h_0 p]$ in $\pi_{33, 37} S^{0,0}/\lambda^2$, detected by $\lambda h_0 p$ on the synthetic Adams $E_\infty$-page. In other words, we have
1409         $$[h_0 h_4^2] \cdot [h_2] = [\lambda h_0 p] \ \text{in} \ \pi_{33, 37} S^{0,0}/\lambda^2.$$
1410 
1411         We will justify this $(f, E_3)$-extension later on in Example~\ref{exam:Mahowald} using the Generalized Mahowald Trick, Theorem~\ref{thm:158d451a}.
1412 
1413         \item For the same map $f$ as in $(1)$, we also have an $(f, E_\infty)$-extension:
1414         $$d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p.$$
1415         This is equivalent of saying that the relation in $\pi_{33, 37} S^{0,0}/\lambda^2$ from $(2)$, can be lifted to a relation
1416         $$[h_0 h_4^2] \cdot [h_2] = [\lambda h_0 p] \ \text{in} \ \pi_{33, 37} S^{0,0},$$
1417         for some classes $[h_0 h_4^2]$ in $\pi_{30+3, 33+4} S^{3,4}$ and $[\lambda h_0 p]$ in $\pi_{33, 37} S^{0,0}$.
1418 
1419         We will justify this $(f, E_\infty)$-extension later on in Example~\ref{exam:stretchext} using Corollary~\ref{cor:dfc6043e}, which allows us to stretch extensions across pages.
1420 
1421         \item Consider $f = 2: S^0 \rightarrow S^0$. We have an $(f, E_\infty)$-extension:
1422         $$d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0 h_3^2) = 0.$$
1423         By definition, $\hat{f} = [h_0]$, detected by $h_0$ in tridegree $(s,t,w) = (1,1,1)$ of the synthetic Adams $E_2$-page. We remark that there is a relation $\lambda \cdot [h_0] = 2$ in $\pi_{0,0}S^{0,0}$. For the synthetic map $[h_0]:S^{0,1} \rightarrow S^{0,0}$,
1424         this $(f, E_\infty)$-extension is equivalent to the existence of a synthetic homotopy class $[h_0 h_3^2]$ in $\pi_{14, 17} S^{0,0}$, such that
1425         $$[h_0 h_3^2] \cdot [h_0] = 0 \ \text{in} \ \pi_{14,18}S^{0,0}.$$
1426         In fact, by inspection, we learn that $\pi_{14,18}S^{0,0} \cong \mathbb{Z}/2$, generated by $[\lambda h_0 d_0]$, which is an $[h_0]$-multiple of a class $[\lambda d_0]$ in a higher Adams filtration than $h_0h_3^2$. Therefore, there exists a class $[h_0 h_3^2]$ whose $[h_0]$-multiple is zero.
1427 
1428         In other words, we have an essential $(f, E_\infty)$-extension:
1429         $$d_1^{f, E_\infty} (d_0) = h_0 d_0,$$
1430         which makes the following alternative $(f, E_\infty)$-extension inessential: 
1431         $$d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0 h_3^2) = h_0 d_0.$$
1432     \end{enumerate}
1433 \end{example}
1434 
1435 
1436 
1437 \begin{definition}\label{def:41d51149}
1438     A crossing of the $(f,E_r)$-extension $d_{n}^{f,E_r}(x)=y$ in (\ref{eq:b98be76a}) is defined as an essential $(f, E_{r-a})$-extension from some $x'\in Z_{r-1-a}^{s+a,t+a}(X)$ to 
1439     $$y'\in Z_{r-1-n+b+e(f)}^{s+n-b,t+n-b}(Y)\backslash B_{1+n-b-e(f)}^{s+n-b,t+n-b}(Y)$$
1440     (where $\backslash$ denotes the difference of sets and it means that $y'$ should survive to the classical Adams $E_{r-n+b+e(f)}$ page while it should not be hit by an Adams differential of length at most $1+n-b-e(f)$) for $0<a \le r-2$ and $0\le b\le n-a-e(f)$. See Figure \ref{fig:66241bf6}.
1441 \end{definition}
1442

1514 
1515 \begin{proposition}\label{prop:cross-f-Er}
1516     An $(f,E_r)$-extension $d_{n}^{f,E_r}(x)=y$ (\ref{eq:b98be76a}) has a crossing if and only if the synthetic $\hat f_{r-1}$-extension $d_{n}^{\hat f_{r-1}}(x)=\lambda^{n-e(f)} y$ (\ref{eq:a020bad3}) has a crossing.
1517 \end{proposition}
1518 
1519 \begin{proof}
1520     By definition a crossing of the synthetic extension (\ref{eq:a020bad3}) has form
1521     \begin{equation}\label{eq:f7a7a418}
1522         d_{n-a-b}^{\hat f_r}(\lambda^ax')=\lambda^{n-b-e(f)}y'
1523     \end{equation}
1524     for some $x'\in Z_{r-1-a}^{s+a,t+a}(X)$ and $y'\in Z_{r-1-n+b+e(f)}^{s+n-b,t+n-b}(Y)$. Consider the following commutative diagram
1525     $$\xymatrix{
1526         \Sus^{0,e(f)}\nu X/\lambda^{r-1-a} \ar[r]^-{\hat f_{r-1-a}} \ar[d]_{\lambda^a} & \nu Y/\lambda^{r-1-a} \ar[d]^{\lambda^a}\\
1527         \Sus^{0,e(f)+a}\nu X/\lambda^{r-1}\ar[r]_-{\hat f_{r-1}} & \Sus^{0,a}\nu Y/\lambda^{r-1}\\
1528     }$$
1529     We see that (\ref{eq:f7a7a418}) lifts to
1530     \begin{equation}\label{eq:5742281}
1531         d_{n-a-b}^{\hat f_{r-1-a}}(x')= \lambda^{n-a-b-e(f)}y''
1532     \end{equation}
1533     for some $y''\in Z_{r-1-n+b+e(f)}^{s+n-b,t+n-b}(Y)$ such that
1534     $$y''\equiv y'\mod B_{1+n-b-e(f)}^{s+n-b,t+n-b}(Y)$$
1535     where the indeterminacy $B_{1+n-b-e(f)}^{s+n-b,t+n-b}(Y)$ is introduced by dividing $\lambda^a$.
1536     Therefore, the differential (\ref{eq:f7a7a418}) is essential if and only if the differential (\ref{eq:5742281}) is essential and $y''\notin B_{1+n-b-e(f)}^{s+n-b,t+n-b}(Y)$. This completes the proof.
1537 \end{proof}
1538 
1539 The above Definition~\ref{def:41d51149}, and Proposition~\ref{prop:cross-f-Er} can be similarly defined and stated for the case $r=\infty$.  
1540 
1541 \begin{example} \label{exam:Ercross}
1542     For the four $(f,E_r)$-extensions in Example~\ref{exam:extonEn}, we consider their crossings.
1543     \begin{enumerate}
1544         \item For $f = \nu: S^3 \rightarrow S^0$, with an $(f, E_2)$-extension:
1545         $$d_1^{f, E_2} (h_5) = h_2 h_5,$$
1546         there are no crossings for degree reasons. In fact, since the map $\hat{f}_1 = [h_2]/\lambda$ has $\AF=1$, $d_1^{\hat{f}_1}$-extensions have no crossings.
1547 
1548         \item For $f = \nu: S^3 \rightarrow S^0$, with an $(f, E_3)$-extension:
1549         $$d_2^{f, E_3} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p,$$
1550         we can verify that it has no crossings from two perspectives.
1551         
1552         First, by Definition~\ref{def:41d51149}, a crossing of this $(f, E_3)$-extension would have $n=2$, $e(f)=1$, which implies $a=1, b=0$. This corresponds to an essential $(f, E_2)$-extension from some 
1553         $$x' \in Z_1^{4,37}(S^3) \cong \Ext_A^{4, 37}(S^3) \cong \mathbb{F}_2,$$
1554         generated by $h_0^2h_4^2$, to some 
1555         $$y' \in Z_1^{5,38}(S^0) \cong \Ext_A^{5, 38}(S^0) \cong \mathbb{F}_2,$$
1556         generated by $h_0p$. However, since in Ext we have
1557         $$h_0^2 h_4^2 \cdot h_2 = 0 \neq h_0 p,$$
1558         this $(f, E_3)$-extension has no crossing.
1559 
1560         Alternatively, as seen in Example~\ref{exam:extonEn}(2), this is equivalent to a synthetic $\hat{f_{2}}$-extension
1561         $$d_2^{\hat{f_{2}}} (h_0h_4^2) = \lambda h_0 p,$$
1562         where 
1563         $$\hat{f_{2}} = [h_2]/\lambda^2: S^{3,4}/\lambda^2 \rightarrow S^{0,0}/\lambda^2.$$
1564         By Proposition~\ref{prop:cross-f-Er}, this $(f, E_3)$-extension has a crossing if and only if the synthetic $\hat{f_{2}}$-extension has a crossing, which is a non-trivial differential of the form
1565         $$d_1^{\hat{f_{2}}} (x') = y'.$$
1566         For degree reasons, the only possible candidates are $$x'= \lambda h_0^2 h_4^2, \ y' = \lambda h_0 p.$$ This again contradicts the relation in Ext, confirming that there is no crossing.
1567 
1568         \item For $f = \nu: S^3 \rightarrow S^0$, with an $(f, E_\infty)$-extension:
1569         $$d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p,$$
1570         we can similarly confirm, using both perspectives outlined in $(2)$, that it has no crossings.
1571 
1572         \item For $f = 2: S^0 \rightarrow S^0$, with an $(f, E_\infty)$-extension:
1573         $$d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0 h_3^2) = 0,$$
1574         the discussion in Example~\ref{exam:extonEn}(4) can be rephrased to indicate a crossing of the form
1575         $$d_1^{f, E_\infty} (d_0) = h_0 d_0.$$
1576         We comment that this $d_1$-differential is also a crossing of the inessential $(f, E_\infty)$-extension:
1577         $$d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0 h_3^2) = h_0 d_0.$$
1578     \end{enumerate}
1579 \end{example}
1580 
1581 \begin{proposition}
1582     Consider $f: X\to Y$, $x\in Z_\infty^{s,t}(X)$ and $y\in Z_\infty^{s+n,t+n}(Y)$.
1583     Then
1584     $$d^{f,E_\infty}_n(x)=y$$
1585     implies that
1586     $$d^{f}_n(x+B_\infty^{s,t}(X))=y+B_\infty^{s,t}(Y).$$
1587 \end{proposition}
1588 (The implied differential could be inessential.)
1589 \begin{proof}
1590   %  By \cite[Proposition 4.40]{Pst} we consider the $\lambda$-inverting functor $\lambda^{-1}:\calS yn\to \calS p$ such that $\lambda^{-1}\circ \nu=id$. The localization map induces a map from the $\hat f$-ESS to the $f$-ESS whose $E_0$ pages are isomorphic to the following
1591   This follows directly from inverting $\lambda$, which induces a map of spectral sequences from the $\hat f$-ESS to the $f$-ESS. The induced map on the $E_0$-pages are of the following form:
1592     $$\xymatrix{
1593     Z_\infty^{s,t}(X)/B_{1+t-w}^{s,t}(X)\ar[r]^-{d^{\hat f}_0}\ar[d]_{\lambda^{-1}} & Z_\infty^{s,t}(Y)/B_{1+t-w-e(f)}^{s,t}(Y)\ar[d]^{\lambda^{-1}}\\
1594     E_\infty^{s,t}(X)\ar[r]^-{d^{f}_0} & E_\infty^{s, t}(Y)
1595     }$$
1596  %   The proposition follows from the naturality of this map between spectral sequences.
1597 \end{proof}
1598 \begin{remark}
1599     Since the synthetic Adams $E_\infty$-page contains more information than the classical Adams $E_\infty$-page, the $(f,E_\infty)$-extensions similarly provide more information compared to the classical $f$-extensions.
1600 \end{remark}
1601 
1602 \section{The Generalized Leibniz Rule and Generalized Mahowald Trick} \label{sec:rules}
1603 
1604 Now, we introduce the theorem of the Generalized Leibniz Rule, a valuable tool for computing new Adams differentials.
1605 
1606 \begin{theorem}[Generalized Leibniz Rule]\label{thm:e73f481e}
1607     Let $f: X\to Y$ be a map between two classical spectra.
1608     Suppose that $2\le n\le r$, $e(f)\le m\le n-2+e(f)$, $l\ge e(f)$, and we have
1609     $$x\in Z_{r-1}^{s,t}(X), \hspace{0.5cm} y\in Z_{r-1-m+e(f)}^{s+m,t+m}(Y)$$
1610     $$x_\infty\in Z_\infty^{s+r,t+r-1}(X), \hspace{0.5cm} y_\infty\in Z_\infty^{s+r+l,t+r+l-1}(Y)$$
1611     and the following conditions hold:
1612     \begin{enumerate}
1613         \item $d_{r}(x)=x_\infty$,
1614         \item $d_{m}^{f,E_n}(x)=y$,
1615         \item $d_{l}^{f,E_\infty}(x_\infty)=y_\infty$,
1616         \item the differential in (1) has no crossing on the $E_n$-page or (2) has no crossing.
1617         \item the differential in (3) has no crossing.% on the $E_\infty$-page.
1618     \end{enumerate}
1619     Then we have an Adams differential
1620     $$d_{r+l-m}(y)= y_\infty.$$
1621 \end{theorem}
1622 
1623 \begin{proof}
1624     Consider the commutative diagram of synthetic spectra
1625     $$\xymatrix{
1626         \Sus^{0,e(f)}\nu X/\lambda^{n-1} \ar[rr]^{\hat f_{n-1}}\ar[d]_{\delta_X} && \nu Y/\lambda^{n-1}\ar[d]_{\delta_Y}\\
1627         \Sus^{1,-n+1+e(f)} \nu X \ar[rr]_{\hat f} && \Sus^{1,-n+1}\nu Y.
1628     }$$
1629     By condition (1) and Proposition \ref{prop:6de7d130}, we have
1630     \begin{equation}\label{eq:ca3fdd92}
1631         d^{\delta_X}_r(x)=\lambda^{r-n}x_\infty.
1632     \end{equation}
1633     By conditions (2) and (3), and Definition \ref{def:6c076a33}, we have
1634     \begin{equation}\label{eq:b2aa5032}
1635         d^{\hat f_{n-1}}_m(x)=\lambda^{m-e(f)} y
1636     \end{equation}
1637     and
1638     \begin{equation*}
1639         d^{\hat f}_l(x_\infty)=\lambda^{l-e(f)} y_\infty, 
1640     \end{equation*}
1641     which implies
1642     \begin{equation}\label{eq:886aee9b}
1643          d^{\hat f}_l(\lambda^{r-n}x_\infty)=\lambda^{r+l-n-e(f)}y_\infty.
1644     \end{equation}
1645     
1646     Applying Proposition \ref{prop:cross-dr-En} and Proposition \ref{prop:cross-f-Er} to conditions (4) and (5), we know that the differential in (\ref{eq:ca3fdd92}) or (\ref{eq:b2aa5032}) has no crossing, and that the differential in (\ref{eq:886aee9b}) has no crossing.
1647     
1648     Therefore, we can apply Corollary \ref{cor:0012nik} and obtain
1649     $$d^{\delta_Y}_{r+l-m}(\lambda^{m-e(f)} y)=\lambda^{r+l-n-e(f)} y_\infty.$$
1650     By Remark \ref{rmk:qvoewfj}, this is equivalent to
1651     $$d_{r+l-m}(y)= y_\infty.$$
1652 \end{proof}
1653 
1654 \begin{remark}
1655     We can further generalize Theorem \ref{thm:e73f481e} by using the conditions in Theorem \ref{thm:4114f70c} rather than those Corollary \ref{cor:0012nik}. We leave this generalization to the reader.
1656 \end{remark}
1657 
1658 We emphasize that the no-crossing conditions in Theorem \ref{thm:e73f481e}, the Generalized Leibniz Rule, are crucial. We first demonstrate the strength of the Generalized Leibniz Rule with Example~\ref{exam:Leibnizyes}, which uses a $d_2$-differential to prove a $d_3$-differential in the classical Adams spectral sequence. Additionally, Example~\ref{exam:Leibnizno} shows that, without the no-crossing condition, the conclusion could be false.
1659 
1660 \begin{remark}
1661     A version of the Generalized Leibniz Rule without the no-crossing conditions is presented in the synthetic setting in Chua's work \cite[Theorem~12.9]{Chua}. However, there is no doubt that this version is incorrect. Indeed, Example~\ref{exam:Leibnizno} is a counter-example to Chua's theorem. For further details, see Remark~\ref{rem:chuaerror}.
1662 \end{remark}
1663 
1664 \begin{example} \label{exam:Leibnizyes}
1665  We show that the Generalized Leibniz Rule can be used to prove the following classical Adams differential
1666     $$d_3(h_2 h_5) = h_0 p,$$
1667     using the classical Hopf invariant one differential
1668     $$d_2(h_5) = h_0 h_4^2,$$
1669     and the $(f, E_3)$-extension
1670         $$d_2^{f, E_3} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p,$$
1671         for the map $f = \nu: S^3 \rightarrow S^0$ from Example~\ref{exam:extonEn}.
1672 
1673         Specifically, we have:
1674         $$n=2, \ r=2, \ s=1, \ t=35, \ m=1, \ l=2, \ e(f)=1,$$
1675         and 
1676         $$x=h_5 \in \Ext_A^{1,32} \cong Z_1^{1,35}(S^3), \ y=h_2h_5 \in \Ext_A^{2,36} \cong Z_1^{2,36}(S^0),$$
1677         $$x_\infty=h_0h_4^2 \in \Ext_A^{3,33} \cong Z_\infty^{3,36}(S^3), \ y_\infty=h_0p \in \Ext_A^{5,38} \cong Z_\infty^{5,38}(S^0).$$
1678 In terms of the conditions in Theorem \ref{thm:e73f481e}, we have:
1679       \begin{enumerate}
1680       \item $d_2(h_5) = h_0h_4^2$,
1681       \item $d_1^{f, E_2}(h_5) = h_2h_5$, from Example~\ref{exam:extonEn}(1),
1682       \item $d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p$, from Example~\ref{exam:extonEn}(3),
1683       \item 
1684       \begin{enumerate}
1685           \item the differential in (1) has no crossing, from Remark~\ref{nocrossE2}, and
1686           \item the differential in (2) has no crossing, from Example~\ref{exam:Ercross}(1), 
1687       \end{enumerate}
1688       \item the differential in (3) has no crossing, from Example~\ref{exam:Ercross}(3).
1689       \end{enumerate}
1690       Since all conditions of Theorem \ref{thm:e73f481e} are satisfied, we conclude that there is a classical Adams differential
1691       $$d_3(h_2 h_5) = h_0 p.$$
1692 \end{example}
1693 
1694 \begin{example} \label{exam:Leibnizno}
1695     We show that, without the no crossing conditions, the Generalized Leibniz Rule could lead to incorrect conclusions. 
1696 
1697     Consider the map $f=2: S^0 \rightarrow S^0$, and the classical Hopf invariant one differential
1698     $$d_2(h_4) = h_0 h_3^2.$$
1699     Set $$n=2, \ r=2, \ s=1, \ t=16, \ m=1, \ l=2, \ e(f)=1,$$
1700         and 
1701         $$x=h_4 \in \Ext_A^{1,16} \cong Z_1^{1,16}(S^0), \ y=h_0h_4 \in \Ext_A^{2,17} \cong Z_1^{2,17}(S^0),$$
1702         $$x_\infty=h_0h_3^2 \in \Ext_A^{3,17} \cong Z_\infty^{3,17}(S^0), \ y_\infty=0 \in \Ext_A^{5,19} \cong Z_\infty^{5,19}(S^0).$$
1703 In terms of the conditions in Theorem \ref{thm:e73f481e}, we have:
1704  \begin{enumerate}
1705       \item $d_2(h_4) = h_0h_3^2$,
1706       \item $d_1^{f, E_2}(h_4) = h_0h_4$, since the product of $h_4$ by $h_0$ is $h_0h_4$ in Ext,
1707       \item $d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0h_3^2) = 0$, from Example~\ref{exam:extonEn}(4),
1708       \item 
1709       \begin{enumerate}
1710           \item the differential in (1) has no crossing, from Remark~\ref{nocrossE2}, and
1711           \item the differential in (2) has no crossing, for degree reasons, 
1712       \end{enumerate}
1713       \item the differential in (3) has a crossing:
1714       $$d_1^{f, E_\infty} (d_0) = h_0 d_0,$$
1715       from Example~\ref{exam:Ercross}(4).
1716       \end{enumerate}
1717       For condition $(5)$, if there were no crossing for the differential in $(3)$, then the Generalized Leibniz Rule would imply a classical Adams differential
1718       $$d_3(h_0h_4) = 0,$$
1719       which is incorrect. This example illustrates that the no-crossing conditions are essential when applying the Generalized Leibniz Rule.
1720 \end{example}
1721 
1722 \begin{remark}
1723     As noted in Example~\ref{exam:extonEn}(4) and Example~\ref{exam:Ercross}(4), the essential $(f, E_\infty)$-extension:
1724         $$d_1^{f, E_\infty} (d_0) = h_0 d_0,$$
1725         is a crossing for both the $(f, E_\infty)$-extension,
1726         $$d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0 h_3^2) = 0,$$
1727         and the inessential $(f, E_\infty)$-extension: 
1728         $$d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0 h_3^2) = h_0 d_0.$$
1729         This indicates that, in Example~\ref{exam:Leibnizno}, if we were to disregard the no-crossing condition (5), and apply the Generalized Leibniz Rule to these two cases of the $(f, E_\infty)$-extensions, we would arrive at two conflicting classical statements:
1730 $$d_3(h_0h_4) = 0, \ d_3(h_0h_4) = h_0 d_0.$$
1731 \end{remark}
1732 
1733 \begin{remark} \label{rem:chuaerror}
1734     In Chua's work \cite[Theorem~12.9]{Chua}, it is stated that for a synthetic map $\alpha: X\rightarrow Y$, and an element $x \in \pi_{*,*}X/\lambda$, there exists a differential from a maximal $\alpha$-extension of $x$ to a maximal $\alpha$-extension of $d_r(x)$. According to  \cite[Definition~12.5]{Chua}, a maximal $\alpha$-extension of $x'$ is defined as $\alpha[x']$, where $[x']$ represents a lift of $x'$ to the $E_{r'}$-page, chosen such that $\alpha[x']$ is the most $\lambda$-divisible among all such lifts. 
1735 
1736     In the context of Example~\ref{exam:Leibnizno}, let $r=2, r'=\infty$,
1737     $$\alpha = [h_0]: S^{0,1} \rightarrow S^{0,0},$$
1738     and consider $x = h_4$ in Ext, with $x' = d_2(x) = h_0h_3^2$.
1739 
1740     As noted in Example~\ref{exam:extonEn}(4), the $(f, E_\infty)$-extension:
1741         $$d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0 h_3^2) = 0$$
1742          is equivalent to the existence of a synthetic homotopy class $[h_0 h_3^2]$ in $\pi_{14, 17} S^{0,0}$, such that
1743         $$[h_0 h_3^2] \cdot [h_0] = 0 \ \text{in} \ \pi_{14,18}S^{0,0}.$$
1744         Similarly, the inessential $(f, E_\infty)$-extension: 
1745         $$d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0 h_3^2) = h_0 d_0$$
1746         implies the existence of another synthetic homotopy class $[h_0 h_3^2]$ in $\pi_{14, 17} S^{0,0}$, such that
1747         $$[h_0 h_3^2] \cdot [h_0] = [h_0d_0] \ \text{in} \ \pi_{14,18}S^{0,0}.$$
1748         Between these two lifts of $h_0h_3^2$, the first $[h_0h_3^2]$ is clearly the maximal $[h_0]$-extension according to Chua’s definition \cite[Definition~12.5]{Chua}. Consequently, the incorrect version of the Generalized Leibniz Rule in \cite[Theorem~12.9]{Chua}, would lead to an incorrect conclusion:
1749         $$d_3(h_0h_4) = 0.$$
1750 \end{remark}
1751 
1752 Next, we discuss the Generalized Mahowald Trick. 
1753 
1754 In order to apply the Generalized Leibniz Rule, we need to provide a method for computing extensions on specific Adams $E_k$-pages, such as the $(f, E_3)$-extension $d_2^{f, E_3} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p$ in Example~\ref{exam:Leibnizyes}. This is provided by Theorem~\ref{thm:158d451a} (the Generalized Mahowald Trick). The crux of the proof of the Generalized Mahowald Trick lies in the following lemma.
1755 \begin{lemma}[May \cite{May01}]\label{lem:452d218c}
1756     Let $X\to Y\to Z\to \Sus X$ and $X'\to Y'\to Z'\to \Sus X'$ be distinguished triangles of (synthetic) spectra. By smashing these distinguished triangles together, we obtain the following commutative diagram of cofiber sequences:
1757     $$\xymatrix{
1758     X\sma X' \ar[r] \ar[d] & Y\sma X' \ar[r] \ar[d] & Z\sma X' \ar[d]\\
1759     X\sma Y' \ar[d] \ar[r] & Y\sma Y' \ar[d] \ar[r] & Z\sma Y' \ar[d]\\
1760     X\sma Z' \ar[r] & Y\sma Z' \ar[r] & Z\sma Z'\\
1761     }$$
1762     If $a\in \pi_n(X\sma Z')$ and $b\in\pi_n(Y\sma Y')$ map to the same element in $\pi_n(Y\sma Z')$, then there exists $c\in \pi_n(Z\sma X')$ such that
1763     \begin{enumerate}
1764         \item $b$ and $c$ map to the same element in $\pi_n(Z\sma Y')$, and
1765         \item $a$ and $c$ map to the same element via boundary maps in $\pi_{n-1}(X\sma X')$.
1766     \end{enumerate}
1767 \end{lemma}
1768 
1769 \begin{proof}
1770 The proof follows directly from \cite[Lemma 4.6]{May01}.
1771 
1772 In fact, consider $V$ in Axiom TC3 of \cite[Section 4]{May01} and the corresponding commutative diagram. By \cite[Lemma 4.6]{May01} we know that $V$ is the pull back of the following diagram.
1773 $$\xymatrix{
1774 \ar@{}[dr]|\lrcorner V\ar[d]\ar[r]& Y\sma Y'\ar[d]\\
1775 X\sma Z'\ar[r] & Y\sma Z'
1776 }$$
1777 Since $a$ and $b$ map to the same element in $\pi_n(Y\sma Z')$, we know that we can find $v\in V$ such that $v$ maps to $a$ in $\pi_n(X\sma Z')$ and $b$ in $\pi_n(Y\sma Y')$. Then we let $c=j_3(v)\in \pi_n(Z\sma X')$, where $j_3: V \rightarrow Z \sma X'$ is the map in the commutative diagram in Axiom TC3 of \cite[Section 4]{May01}. This lemma follows from the commutativity of the diagram.
1778 \end{proof}
1779  
1780 \begin{theorem}[Generalized Mahowald Trick]\label{thm:158d451a} 
1781     Consider a distinguished triangle of spectra
1782     $$X\fto{f} Y\fto{g} Z\fto{h} \Sus X$$
1783     with $e(f)+e(g)+e(h)=1$.
1784     Suppose that $r=n+m+l$, $n_1=n-e(f)\ge 1$, $m_1=m-e(g)\ge 0$, $l_1=l-e(h)\ge 0$, and
1785     $$\begin{array}{ll}
1786         x\in Z_{n_1}^{s+l,t+l-1}(X), & y\in Z_{m_1+1}^{s+n+l,t+n+l-1}(Y),\\
1787         \bar x\in Z_{r-1}^{s,t}(Z), & \bar y\in Z_\infty^{s+r,t+r-1}(Z),
1788     \end{array}$$
1789     such that
1790     \begin{enumerate}
1791         \item $d^{h,E_{r'}}_{l}\bar x=x$, where $r'=r-m_1=n_1+l_1+1$,
1792         \item $d_r \bar x=\bar y$,
1793             \item the $(h,E_{r'})$-extension in (1) has no crossing, or the Adams differential (2) has no crossing on the $E_{r'}$-page,
1794         \item $d^{g,E_{m_1+2}}_{m}y=\bar y$.
1795     \end{enumerate}
1796     Then we have $x\in Z_{n+m+e(h)}^{s+l,t+l-1}(X)$ and
1797     $$d^{f,E_{n+m+1+e(h)}}_{n}x\equiv y \mod B_{r'}^{s+n+l,t+n+l-1}(Y).$$
1798     (See Figure \ref{fig:2aa45b21}.)
1799 \end{theorem}

1847

1852         S^{0,0}/\lambda^{r} \ar[d]_{\rho} & & & & [\lambda^{l_1}x] \ar[d]\\

1854         S^{1,-r'+1}/\lambda^{m_1+1} \ar[d]_{\lambda^{r'-1}} & & [y] \ar[r] \ar[d] & [\lambda^{m_1}\bar y] & \\

1859 
1860 \begin{proof}
1861     Consider the following two distinguished triangles of synthetic spectra
1862     \begin{gather*}
1863         \nu X\fto{\hat f} \Sus^{0,-e(f)}\nu Y\fto{\hat g} \Sus^{0,-e(f)-e(g)}\nu Z\fto{\hat h} \Sus^{0,-1}\nu \Sus X=\Sus^{1,0}\nu X\\
1864         S^{0,0}/\lambda^{r}\fto{\rho} S^{0,0}/\lambda^{r'-1} \fto{\delta_{r'-1}} S^{1,-r'+1}/\lambda^{m_1+1} \fto{\lambda^{r'-1}} S^{1,0}/\lambda^{r}
1865     \end{gather*}
1866     and their smash product. See Figure \ref{fig:2d013742}.
1867 
1868     By condition (1), we have
1869     \begin{equation}\label{eq:70327019}
1870         d^{\hat h_{r'-1}}_l(\bar x)=\lambda^{l_1}x
1871     \end{equation}
1872     where $\hat h_{r'-1}$ is the map $\Sus^{0,e(h)}\nu Z/\lambda^{r'-1}\to \nu X/\lambda^{r'-1}$ induced by $\hat h$.
1873     By condition (2), we have
1874     \begin{equation}\label{eq:e129d90c}
1875         d^{\delta_{r'-1}}_r(\bar x)=\lambda^{m_1}\bar y.
1876     \end{equation}
1877  
1878     
1879     Applying Proposition \ref{prop:cross-dr-En} and Proposition \ref{prop:cross-f-Er} to condition (3), we know that the differential in (\ref{eq:70327019}) or the differential in (\ref{eq:e129d90c}) has no crossing. Hence, there exists
1880     $$[\bar x]\in \{\bar x\}\subset \pi_{t-s,t}(\nu Z/\lambda^{r'-1})$$
1881     such that
1882     $$\hat h_{r'-1}([\bar x])\in \{\lambda^{l_1}x\}\subset \pi_{t-s-1, t-1+e(h)}(\nu X/\lambda^{r'-1})$$
1883     and
1884     $$\delta_{r'-1}([\bar x])\in \{\lambda^{m_1}\bar y\}\subset \pi_{t-s-1,t+r'-1}(\nu Z/\lambda^{m_1+1}).$$
1885 
1886     By condition (4), we have
1887     \begin{equation*}
1888         d^{\hat g_{m_1+1}}_m(y)=\lambda^{m_1}\bar y.
1889     \end{equation*}
1890     This implies that there exists
1891     $$[y]\in \{y\}\subset \pi_{t-s-1,t+n+l-1}(\nu Y/\lambda^{m_1+1})$$
1892     such that
1893     $$\hat g_{m_1+1}([y])\in \{\lambda^{m_1}\bar y\}\subset \pi_{t-s-1, t+r'-1}(\nu Z/\lambda^{m_1+1}),$$
1894     where $\hat g_{m_1+1}$ is the map $\Sus^{0,e(g)}\nu Y/\lambda^{m_1+1}\to \nu Z/\lambda^{m_1+1}$ induced by $\hat g$.
1895     
1896     Due to degree reasons (Proposition \ref{prop:59f111f}), $\lambda^{m_1}\bar y$ detects a unique element in homotopy,
1897     $$[\lambda^{m_1}\bar y]\in \pi_{t-s-1, t+r'-1}(\nu Z)/\lambda^{m_1+1}.$$
1898     Therefore, we have
1899     $$\delta_{r'-1}([\bar x])=\hat g_{m_1+1}([y])=[\lambda^{m_1}\bar y],$$
1900     By Lemma \ref{lem:452d218c}, there exists
1901     $$[\lambda^{l_1}x]\in \pi_{t-s-1,t-1+e(h)}(\nu X/\lambda^r),$$
1902     such that
1903     \begin{gather}
1904         \rho ([\lambda^{l_1}x])=\hat h_{r'-1}([\bar x]),\label{eq:f4848f29}\\
1905         \hat f_r([\lambda^{l_1}x])=\lambda^{r'-1}[y],\label{eq:fba35e4e}
1906     \end{gather}
1907     where $\rho$ is the map $\nu X/\lambda^r\to \nu X/\lambda^{r'-1}$, and $\hat f_r$ is the map $\Sus^{0,e(f)}\nu X/\lambda^r\to \nu Y/\lambda^r$ induced by $\hat f$.
1908     
1909     The equality in (\ref{eq:f4848f29}) indicates that $[\lambda^{l_1}x]$ can be lifted to the $E_\infty$-page of $\nu X/\lambda^r$, so we have
1910     $$x\in Z_{n+m+e(h)}^{s+l,t+l-1}(X).$$
1911     The equation in (\ref{eq:fba35e4e}) indicates that
1912     $$d^{\hat f_r}_n(\lambda^{l_1}x)=\lambda^{r'-1}y.$$
1913     Therefore, by dividing $\lambda^{l_1}$, we have
1914     $$d^{\hat f_{n+m+e(h)}}_n(x)\equiv \lambda^{n_1}y \mod B_{r'}^{s+n+l,t+n+l-1}(Y)$$
1915     for $x$ in the $E_\infty$ page of $\nu X/\lambda^{r-l_1}=\nu X/\lambda^{n+m+e(h)}$. By definition, this is equivalent to
1916     $$d^{f, E_{n+m+1+e(h)}}_nx\equiv y \mod B_{r'}^{s+n+l,t+n+l-1}(Y).$$
1917 \end{proof}
1918 
1919 
1920     We refer to Theorem~\ref{thm:158d451a} as the Generalized Mahowald Trick, as this approach was first utilized by Mahowald and his collaborators in various works (see, for example, \cite{MahowaldTangora}), particularly in the case where $m_1 = l_1 = 0$. The synthetic setting advances this method by allowing for the consideration of cases where $m_1>0, l_1>0$ as well. 
1921     
1922 \begin{remark}
1923     Several versions of classical generalizations of Mahowald's original trick appear in the literature and are often referred to as geometric boundary theorems. Notable examples include the works of Behrens \cite{Behrens}, and Ma \cite{Ma}.
1924 \end{remark}
1925 
1926 \begin{remark}
1927   A synthetic generalization of the Mahowald Trick, again lacking any no-crossing conditions, is also presented in Chua's work \cite[Theorem~12.11]{Chua}. This version is also incorrect for similar reasons.
1928 \end{remark}
1929 
1930 \begin{example} \label{exam:Mahowald}
1931     We apply the Generalized Mahowald Trick to prove the claim in Example~\ref{exam:extonEn}(2) regarding an $(f, E_3)$-extension:
1932         $$d_2^{f, E_3} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p,$$
1933         for the map $f=\nu:S^3 \rightarrow S^0$.
1934 
1935         Specifically, let $X = S^3, Y = S^0, Z = S^0/\nu$, where $Z$ is the cofiber of $\nu \in \pi_3$, we have a distinguished triangle
1936         $$\xymatrix{S^3 \ar[r]^{\nu} & S^0 \ar[r]^-{i} & S^0/\nu \ar[r]^{q} & S^4.}$$
1937 We set
1938 $$f=\nu, \ g=i, \ h=q, \ e(f)=1, \ e(g)=0, \ e(h)=0.$$
1939 The short exact sequence on $\HF$-homology
1940 $$\xymatrix{0 \ar[r] & {\HF}_*S^0 \ar[r]^-{i_*} & {\HF}_*S^0/\nu \ar[r]^-{q_*} & {\HF}_*S^4 \ar[r] & 0,}$$
1941 induces a long exact sequence on Ext-groups
1942 $$\xymatrix{\cdots \ar[r]^-{\cdot h_2} & {\Ext}_A^{*,*}(S^0) \ar[r]^-{i_*} & {\Ext}_A^{*,*}(S^0/\nu) \ar[r]^-{q_*} & {\Ext}_A^{*,*}(S^4) \ar[r]^-{\cdot h_2} & \cdots}.$$
1943 We use Atiyah--Hirzebruch notation (as in \cite[Notation 3.3]{WX61, WangXu51ext}) to denote elements in ${\Ext}_A^{*,*}(S^0/\nu)$. Specifically, if an element $x$ in ${\Ext}_A^{*,*}(S^0/\nu)$ satisfies 
1944 $$q_*(x) = a \neq 0 \in \Ext_A^{*,*}(S^4),$$ 
1945 we denote $x$ by $a[4]$; otherwise, due to exactness, 
1946 $$x = i_*(b) \ \text{for some} \ b \in \Ext_A^{*,*}(S^0),$$
1947 and in this case, we denote $x$ by $b[0]$. 
1948 
1949 We have
1950 $$m=0, \ l=0, \ n=2, \ r=2, \ r'=2, \ n_1=1, \ m_1=0, \ l_1=0,$$
1951 and
1952 
1953 % \begin{equation*}
1954 \begin{align*}
1955 x & =h_0h_4^2 && \in \Ext_A^{3,33} && \cong Z_1^{3,36}(S^3),\\
1956 \bar{x} & =h_0h_4^2[4] && \in \Ext_A^{3,37}(S^0/\nu) && \cong Z_1^{3,37}(S^0/\nu), \\
1957 y & =h_0p && \in \Ext_A^{5,38} && \cong Z_1^{5,38}(S^0),\\
1958         \bar{y} & =h_0p[0] && \in \Ext_A^{5,38}(S^0/\nu) && \cong Z_\infty^{5,38}(S^0/\nu). 
1959   \end{align*}      
1960 %  \end{equation*}
1961 For conditions in Theorem~\ref{thm:158d451a}, we have:
1962 \begin{enumerate}
1963       \item $d_0^{h, E_2}(h_0h_4^2[4]) = h_0h_4^2$, since in Ext $h_2 \cdot h_0h_4^2 = 0$.
1964       \item $d_2(h_0h_4^2[4]) = h_0p[0]$. This is a classical Adams $d_2$-differential for $S^0/\nu$, and can be computed using Lin's computer program via the method of the secondary Steenrod algebra \cite{BJ, Nassau, Chua}.
1965       \item 
1966       \begin{enumerate}
1967           \item The differential in (1) has no crossing, as it is a $d_0$-differential, and
1968           \item the differential in (2) has no crossing, from Remark~\ref{nocrossE2}. 
1969       \end{enumerate}
1970       \item 
1971       $d_0^{g, E_2} (h_0p) = h_0p[0]$, as $h_0p$ is not divisible by $h_2$ in Ext.
1972       \end{enumerate}
1973     Since all conditions of  Theorem~\ref{thm:158d451a} are  satisfied, we conclude that $x=h_0h_4^2$ survives to the $E_3$-page, and there is an $(f, E_3)$-extension:
1974         $$d_2^{f, E_3} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p,$$
1975         as promised.
1976         
1977 \end{example}
1978 
1979 The outcome of the Generalized Mahowald Trick, as stated in Theorem~\ref{thm:158d451a}, is an $f$-extension on a specific Adams page, such as the $(f, E_3)$-extension $d_2^{f, E_3} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p$ in Example~\ref{exam:extonEn}(2) and Example~\ref{exam:Mahowald}. In practice, the source of an $(f, E_r)$-extension may survive to later Adams pages, prompting interest in the $(f, E_{>r})$-extensions, such as the $(f, E_\infty)$-extension $d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p$ in Example~\ref{exam:extonEn}(3), which is applied in Example~\ref{exam:Leibnizyes}. The following Propositions~\ref{prop:dec738d3} and \ref{cor:dfc6043e} describe the relationships between extensions across different pages.
1980 
1981 \begin{proposition}\label{prop:dec738d3}
1982     Suppose that we have an $(f,E_r)$-extension $d^{f,E_r}_n(x)=y$, where $x\in Z_{r-1}^{s,t}(X)$ and $y\in Z_{r-1-n+e(f)}^{s+n,t+n}(Y)$. Then for all $2\le r'<r$, we also have
1983     \begin{equation}\label{eq:dr'}
1984         d^{f,E_{r'}}_n(x)=y.
1985     \end{equation}
1986 
1987     Furthermore, if $d^{f,E_r}_n(x)=y$ is essential and $n\le r'-2+e(f)$, then (\ref{eq:dr'}) is inessential if and only if there exists some $0<a'\le n-e(f)$ and an element
1988     $$x'\in Z_{r'-1-a'}^{s+a',t+a'}(X)\backslash Z_{r-1-a'}^{s+a',t+a'}(X)$$
1989     such that
1990     $$d^{\hat f_{r'-1}}_{n-a'-b}(\lambda^{a'} x')=\lambda^{n-b-e(f)} y'$$
1991     for some $b\ge 0$.
1992     
1993 \end{proposition}
1994 
1995 \begin{proof}
1996     Consider the following commutative diagram:
1997     $$\xymatrix{
1998         \Sus^{0,e(f)}\nu X/\lambda^{r-1} \ar[r]^-{\hat f_{r-1}}\ar[d]_{\rho_X} & \nu Y/\lambda^{r-1} \ar[d]_{\rho_Y}\\
1999         \Sus^{0,e(f)}\nu X/\lambda^{r'-1} \ar[r]^-{\hat f_{r'-1}} & \nu Y/\lambda^{r'-1}
2000     }$$
2001     By Corollary \ref{cor:e7b20ae2}, $(\rho_X,\rho_Y)$ induces a map from the $\hat f_{r-1}$-ESS to the $\hat f_{r'-1}$-ESS. Therefore, by naturality,
2002     $$d^{\hat f_{r-1}}_n(x)=\lambda^{n-e(f)} y\ \text{implies} \ d^{\hat f_{r'-1}}_n(x)=\lambda^{n-e(f)} y,$$
2003     which, by definition, is
2004     $$d^{f,E_{r'}}_n(x)=y.$$
2005    
2006     Next, we prove the second part of the proposition. By Definition \ref{def:6c076a33}, the $(f,E_r)$-extension (\ref{eq:dr'}) is inessential if and only if 
2007     there exists $0<a\le n-e(f)$ and 
2008     $$\lambda^a x'\in E_\infty^{s+a,t+a,t}(\nu X/\lambda^{r'-1})\iso Z_{r'-1-a}^{s+a,t+a}(X)/B_{1+a}^{s+a,t+a}(X),$$
2009     such that
2010     \begin{equation}\label{eq:dcd62a19}
2011         d^{\hat f_{r'-1}}_{n-a}(\lambda^ax')=\lambda^{n-e(f)} y,
2012     \end{equation}
2013     and this differential is not induced by $(\rho_X,\rho_Y)$, as we assume that $d^{f,E_r}_n(x)=y$ is essential.
2014     
2015     There are two scenarios where this differential is not induced by $(\rho_X,\rho_Y)$. The first case occurs when $\lambda^ax'$ is not in the image of $\rho_X$ at all, which is equivalent to $x'\notin Z_{r-1-a}^{s+a,t+a}(X)$. (See Figure \ref{fig:b22d961f}.) 
2016     
2017     \begin{figure}[h]
2018     \centering
2019     \scalebox{0.9}{\begin{tikzpicture}
2020         % Draw grid
2021         \fill[llgray] (0-0.5, 4-0.5) rectangle ++(1, 2);
2022         \tikzgrid{0}{2}{0}{7}
2023         \tikzgrid{3}{5}{0}{7}
2024         \draw[thick] (0-0.5, 4-0.5) rectangle ++(1, 2);
2025     
2026         % Draw bullets
2027         \coordinate (x) at (1, 0);
2028         \coordinate (x1) at (1, 1);
2029         \coordinate (dx1) at (0, 5);
2030         \coordinate (dx) at (0, 6);
2031         \coordinate (y) at (4, 3);
2032         \coordinate (y1) at (4, 2);
2033         \coordinate (dy) at (3, 6);
2034         
2035         \fill (x) circle (0.1) node[below right] {$x$};
2036         \fill (x1) circle (0.1) node[below right] {$x'$};
2037         \fill (y1) circle (0.1) node[right] {$y$};
2038         \fill (dx1) circle (0.1) node[right] {};
2039     
2040         % Draw differentials
2041         \draw[dashed, -{Stealth[length=1.5mm]}, shorten >=(0.08cm)] (x) -- (dx) node[near end, above right] {$d_{\ge r'}$};
2042         \drawarrow (x1) -- (dx1) node[near end,left
2043         ] {$d_*$};
2044         \draw[dashed, -{Stealth[length=1.5mm]}, shorten >=(0.08cm)] (x) -- (y) node[near end,above left=-5pt] {$d^{f,E_{r'}}_{>n}$};
2045         \drawarrow (x) -- (y1) node[midway,below right=-5pt] {$d^{f,E_{r}}_{n}$};
2046         \drawarrow (x1) -- (y1) node[near start,above=-3pt] {$d^{f,E_{r'-a}}_{n-a}$};
2047

2050         \node[anchor=east] at (-0.6, 2) {$s+n$};
2051         \node[anchor=east] at (-0.6, 4) {$s+r'$};
2052         \node[anchor=east] at (-0.6, 6) {$s+r$};
2053     
2054         \node at (.5, -1.0) {$E_2(X)$};
2055         \node at (3.5, -1.0) {$E_2(Y)$};
2056     \end{tikzpicture}}
2057     \caption{Extensions across pages}\label{fig:b22d961f}
2058     \end{figure}
2059     
2060     The second case occurs when $\lambda^ax'$ is in the image of $\rho_X$, but it supports an essential $\hat f_{r-1}$-extension that is strictly shorter than the differential (\ref{eq:dcd62a19}). 
2061     
2062     To further explore this scenario, we replace $x$ with $\lambda^ax'$ and analyze successive essential extensions. This process is repeated iteratively until the first case is reached. Ultimately, this leads to the existence of some $0<a'\le n-e(f)$ and an element
2063     $$x''\in Z_{r'-1-a'}^{s+a',t+a'}(X)\backslash Z_{r-1-a'}^{s+a',t+a'}(X)$$
2064     such that
2065     $$d^{\hat f_{r'-1}}_{n-a'-b}(\lambda^{a'} x'')=\lambda^{n-b-e(f)} y'$$
2066     for some $b>0$. This represents a crossing of $d^{\hat f_{r'-1}}_n(x)=\lambda^{n-e(f)}y$.
2067     
2068 \end{proof}
2069 
2070 
2071 The following Corollary~\ref{cor:dfc6043e} is the contrapositive statement of the second part of Proposition~\ref{prop:dec738d3}.
2072 
2073 
2074 \begin{corollary}\label{cor:dfc6043e}
2075     Suppose $r\ge r'$ and there exists an $(f,E_{r'})$-extension 
2076     $$d^{f,E_{r'}}_n(x)=y$$
2077     for $x\in Z_{r-1}^{s,t}(X)$ and $y\in Z_{r'-1-n+e(f)}^{s+n,t+n}(Y)$. Assume that this extension has no crossing of the form $d^{f,E_{r'-a}}_{n-a-b}(x')=y'$ for any $b>0$, $0<a\le n-e(f)$, and 
2078     $$x'\in Z_{r'-1-a}^{s+a,t+a}(X)\backslash Z_{r-1-a}^{s+a,t+a}(X).$$ 
2079     Under these conditions, we also have an $(f,E_{r})$-extension
2080     $$d^{f,E_{r}}_n(x)=y.$$
2081 \end{corollary}
2082 
2083 
2084 \begin{example} \label{exam:stretchext}
2085     In Example~\ref{exam:Mahowald}, we use the Generalized Mahowald Trick to establish the $(f, E_3)$-extension:
2086         $$d_2^{f, E_3} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p,$$
2087         for the map $f=\nu:S^3 \rightarrow S^0$, as discussed
2088 in Example~\ref{exam:extonEn}(2). However, to apply the Generalized Leibniz Rule in proving the classical Adams differential 
2089 $$d_3(h_2h_5) = h_0p$$
2090 in Example~\ref{exam:Leibnizyes}, we need the $E_\infty$-page version of the $(f, E_3)$-extension:
2091 $$d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p.$$
2092 We apply Corollary~\ref{cor:dfc6043e} to confirm this.
2093 
2094 %Specifically, set 
2095 %$$r'=3, \ r=\infty, \ n=2, \ s=3, \ t=36, \ e(f)=1,$$
2096 %$$x=h_0h_4^2 \in Z_\infty^{3,36}(S^3), \ y = h_0p \in Z_1^{5,38}(S^0)$$
2097 As checked in Example~\ref{exam:Ercross}(2), the $(f, E_3)$-extension has no crossing. Consequently, by Corollary~\ref{cor:dfc6043e}, we obtain the required $(f, E_\infty)$-extension:
2098 $$d_2^{f, E_\infty} (h_0h_4^2) = h_0 p.$$
2099 \end{example}

2101 
2102 \section{Proof of the main theorem}
2103 
2104 In this section, we present the proof of our main Theorem~\ref{thm:h_6_sq}. 
2105 
2106 \begin{theorem}[Theorem~\ref{thm:h_6_sq}] \label{thm:126survives}
2107     The element $h_6^2$ survives to the $E_\infty$-page in the Adams spectral sequence.
2108 \end{theorem}
2109 
2110 We first recall the following theorem, known as the inductive method, originally by Barratt--Jones--Mahowald \cite{BJMinduction} and later extended to the H$\mathbb{F}_2$-synthetic setting by Burklund--Xu \cite[Proposition~7.19]{BurklundXu}.
2111 
2112 \begin{notation}
2113     Let $\theta_5 = [h_5^2]$ represent any synthetic homotopy class in $\pi_{62,62+2}S^{0,0}$ detected by $h_5^2$ in the Adams $E_2$-page. For convenience, we use the same notation, $\theta_5$, to denote its image in $\pi_{62,62+2}S^{0,0}/\lambda^r$ via the map $S^{0,0} \rightarrow S^{0,0}/\lambda^r$ for all $r \ge 1$. Similarly, let $\eta = [h_1] \in \pi_{1,1+1}S^{0,0}$.
2114 \end{notation}
2115 
2116 
2117 \begin{theorem}[Barratt--Jones--Mahowald, Burklund--Xu] \label{thm:bjmbx}
2118 \begin{enumerate}
2119  \item[]
2120  \item    The element $h_6^2$ survives to the $E_{r+3}$-page of the classical Adams spectral sequence if and only if for some $\theta_5$, 
2121  $$\lambda \eta \theta_5^2 = 0 \ \text{in} \ \pi_{125,125+4} S^{0,0}/\lambda^{r+1}.$$
2122  \item   In particular, $h_6^2$ is a permanent cycle in the classical Adams spectral sequence if and only if for some $\theta_5$,
2123  $$\lambda \eta \theta_5^2 = 0 \ \text{in} \ \pi_{125,125+4} S^{0,0}.$$ 
2124  \end{enumerate}
2125 \end{theorem}
2126 
2127 \begin{remark}
2128     The statement in Theorem~\ref{thm:bjmbx}(1) was originally stated as 
2129     $$\eta \theta_5^2 = 0 \ \text{in} \ \pi_{125,125+5} S^{0,0}/\lambda^r$$
2130     in \cite[Proposition~7.19]{BurklundXu}. Upon inspection, $\pi_{125,125+5} S^{0,0}$ doesn't contain any $\lambda$-torsion classes, so this is equivalent to the version we stated, which is more consistent with the statement in part (2). 
2131 \end{remark}
2132 
2133 \begin{remark} \label{rem:theta5choice}
2134     Theorem~\ref{thm:bjmbx} is proved using the quadratic construction  on a map from the mod 2 Moore spectrum to the sphere spectrum, where the restriction on the bottom cell is $\theta_5$. Therefore, it is necessary to use a $\theta_5$ of order 2. 
2135     
2136     Notably, \cite{Xu, IWX} confirms that all classical $\theta_5$'s indeed have order 2. Furthermore, from Proposition~\ref{prop:30e8b746} and an analysis of the differentials in the classical Adams spectral sequence, we find that $\pi_{62,62+2}S^{0,0}$ doesn't contain any $\lambda$-torsion. Consequently, all synthetic $\theta_5$'s also have order 2, making it valid to apply Theorem~\ref{thm:bjmbx} to any $\theta_5$. 
2137     
2138     Additionally, since the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:bjmbx} shows that the expression $\lambda \eta \theta_5^2$ corresponds to the total differential $\delta_1: S^{0,0}/\lambda \rightarrow S^{1,-1}$ on $h_6^2$, the value of the expression $\lambda \eta \theta_5^2$ is consistent for every choice of $\theta_5$.  (Note that our grading for the triangulation translation functor is smashing with $S^{1,0}$, which is consistent with \cite[Appendix A]{BHS} but is different from \cite[Section 7]{BurklundXu}, so the target of $\delta_1$ is $S^{1,-1}$. )
2139 \end{remark}
2140 
2141 We pay special attention to the following three elements on the classical Adams $E_2$-page (see Figure~\ref{Fig:near126} and Tables~\ref{Table:S125.19}, \ref{Table:S126.10}, \ref{Table:S124.12}, \ref{Table:S125.20} in the Appendix): 
2142 \begin{align*}
2143     h_1h_4x_{109,12} & \in \Ext_A^{14, 125+14}, \\
2144     x_{126,8,4} + x_{126,8} & \in \Ext_A^{8, 126+8}, \\
2145     h_0^2x_{124,8} & \in \Ext_A^{10, 124+10},\\
2146     g^4\Delta h_1g & \in \Ext_A^{25,125+25}.
2147 \end{align*}
2148 
2149 For the right side of Figure ~\ref{Fig:near126}, we use dashed differentials to indicate the shortest possible nonzero differentials that these elements could support.
2150 
2151 {
2152 \include{112}
2153 }
2154 
2155 From Figure~\ref{Fig:near126} and Tables~\ref{Table:S124.12}, \ref{Table:S126.10}, \ref{Table:S125.19}, \ref{Table:S125.20} in the Appendix, we know that
2156 \begin{fact} \label{fact:theta5sqAF}
2157 \begin{enumerate}
2158    % \item[]
2159     \item $x_{126,8,4} + x_{126,8}$ survives to the $E_6$-page.
2160     
2161     \item $h_1h_4x_{109,12}$ is a permanent cycle, and can only be killed by 
2162     $$d_6(x_{126,8,4} + x_{126,8}) \ \text{or} \ d_{12}(h_6^2).$$
2163     
2164     \item $h_0^2x_{124,8}$ survives to the $E_\infty$-page.
2165 
2166     \item In $\Ext_A^{25,125+25}$, the element $g^4\Delta h_1g$ is the only one that survives to the classical $E_5$-page.
2167 \end{enumerate}\end{fact}
2168 
2169 \begin{remark}
2170 From Table~\ref{Table:S126.10} in the Appendix, we have
2171   % For Fact~\ref{fact:theta5sqAF}(2), the element $x_{126,6} \in \Ext_A^{6, 126+6}$ supports a nonzero $d_3$-differential and therefore cannot kill $h_1h_4x_{109,12}$. In fact, since 
2172  %  $$h_2 \cdot d_3(x_{126,6}) = d_3(h_2 \cdot x_{126,6}) = h_0^2 h_3 D_2 h_6 \neq 0 
2173  %  \ \text{on the} \ E_3\text{-page},$$
2174  %  we have 
2175    $$d_3(x_{126,6}) = h_5x_{94,8}, \ \text{or} \ h_5x_{94,8} + h_6(\Delta e_1 + C_0 + h_0^6h_5^2) \neq 0.$$
2176    Therefore, the element $x_{126,6} \in \Ext_A^{6, 126+6}$ cannot kill $h_1h_4x_{109,12}$.
2177 \end{remark}
2178 
2179 We will apply Theorem~\ref{thm:bjmbx} to prove the following Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq}.
2180 
2181 \begin{proposition} \label{prop:possible_h_6_sq}\label{prop:possibleh62}
2182 Exactly one of the following two statements is true:
2183 \begin{enumerate}
2184     \item The element $h_6^2$ survives to the $E_\infty$-page.
2185    
2186     \item There is a nonzero classical Adams differential 
2187     $$d_{12}(h_6^2) = h_1h_4x_{109,12}.$$
2188 \end{enumerate}
2189 Furthermore, statement $(2)$ is true if and only if the following three statements are all true:
2190 \begin{enumerate} \setcounter{enumi}{2}
2191     \item \leavevmode\vspace*{-\dimexpr\abovedisplayskip + \baselineskip}  
2192     $$d_6(x_{126,8,4} + x_{126,8}) = 0.$$
2193     
2194     \item There exists a $\theta_5$ such that 
2195     $\theta_5^2$ is detected by $\lambda^6 h_0^2x_{124,8}$. In particular, 
2196     $$\theta_5^2 = \lambda^6 [h_0^2x_{124,8}] \neq 0 \in \pi_{124,124+4}S^{0,0}$$
2197     for some $[h_0^2x_{124,8}]$.
2198 
2199     \item There exists a homotopy class $[h_0^2x_{124,8}]$ such that
2200     $\lambda^3 \eta [h_0^2x_{124,8}]$ is detected by $\lambda^6 h_1h_4x_{109,12}$. In particular, we have 
2201     $$\lambda^3 \eta [h_0^2x_{124,8}] = \lambda^6 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}] \in \pi_{125,125+8} S^{0,0}$$
2202     for some $[h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$.
2203 \end{enumerate}
2204 \end{proposition}
2205 
2206 By further analyzing classical Adams differentials, we reduce the $\eta$-extension in statement $(5)$ of Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} to a specific 2-extension in stem 125 (Corollary~\ref{cor:2ext125}), and then compare it with a particular $\nu$-extension in stem 125 (Lemma~\ref{lem:nuext125}) to demonstrate that the 2-extension cannot hold. This ultimately leads to the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:state5false}.
2207 
2208 \begin{proposition} \label{prop:state5false}
2209     If statement $(3)$ is true, then statement $(5)$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} must be false.
2210 \end{proposition}
2211 
2212 \begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:126survives}]
2213     From Proposition~\ref{prop:state5false}, at least one of  statements $(3)$ or $(5)$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} is false. Consequently,  statement $(2)$ is also false, which confirms that statement $(1)$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} is true.  
2214 \end{proof}
2215 
2216 In the rest of this section, we prove Propositions~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} and \ref{prop:state5false}.\\
2217 
2218 To prove Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq}, we first establish Lemmas~\ref{lem:equistate4} and \ref{lem:equistate5}, which demonstrate that the existential statements $(4)$ and $(5)$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} are equivalent to their corresponding universal statements $(4')$ and $(5')$. 
2219 
2220 %\usepackage{enumitem}
2221 \begin{lemma} \label{lem:equistate4}
2222     The statement $(4)$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} is equivalent to the following statement $(4')$:
2223     \begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*')]\setcounter{enumi}{3} 
2224     \item For every $\theta_5$, we have $\theta_5^2$ is detected by $\lambda^6 h_0^2x_{124,8}$. In particular, 
2225     $$\theta_5^2 = \lambda^6 [h_0^2x_{124,8}] \neq 0 \in \pi_{124,124+4}S^{0,0}$$
2226     for some $[h_0^2x_{124,8}]$.
2227     \end{enumerate}
2228 \end{lemma}
2229 
2230 \begin{proof}
2231     We only need to show that statement $(4)$ implies statement $(4')$. 
2232     
2233     According to \cite{IWX}, 
2234     $$\pi_{62} \cong \mathbb{Z}/2 \oplus \mathbb{Z}/2 \oplus \mathbb{Z}/2 \oplus \mathbb{Z}/2,$$ 
2235     and is generated by $\theta_5$ and classes of $\AF=6,8,10$. Therefore, the indeterminacy of the classical $\theta_5$, or the difference of any two choices of classical $\theta_5$, lies in $\AF \ge 6$.
2236     
2237     As explained in Remark~\ref{rem:theta5choice}, $\pi_{62,62+2}S^{0,0}$ contains no $\lambda$-torsion, and therefore, the indeterminacy of the synthetic $\theta_5$ also belongs to $\AF \ge 6$. Since every $\theta_5$ has order 2, the indeterminacy of the synthetic $\theta_5^2$ lies in $\AF \ge 12$.
2238 
2239     Therefore, if for some $\theta_5$, $\theta_5^2$ is detected by this specific element $\lambda^6 h_0^2x_{124,8}$, which is nonzero in $\AF=10$, then for any $\theta_5$, $\theta_5^2$ is nonzero and detected by $\lambda^6 h_0^2x_{124,8}$.
2240 
2241 \end{proof}
2242 
2243 
2244 \begin{lemma} \label{lem:equistate5}
2245     The statement $(5)$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} is equivalent to the following statement $(5')$:
2246     \begin{enumerate}[label=(\arabic*')]\setcounter{enumi}{4} 
2247     \item For every homotopy class $[h_0^2x_{124,8}]$, we have
2248     $\lambda^3 \eta [h_0^2x_{124,8}]$ is detected by $\lambda^6 h_1h_4x_{109,12}$. In particular, we have 
2249     $$\lambda^3 \eta [h_0^2x_{124,8}] = \lambda^6 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}] \in \pi_{125,125+8} S^{0,0}$$
2250     for some $[h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$.
2251     \end{enumerate}
2252 \end{lemma}
2253 
2254 \begin{proof}
2255     We only need to show that statement $(5)$ implies statement $(5')$, which is sufficient to show that the indeterminacy of $[h_0^2x_{124,8}]$, or the difference between any two homotopy classes $[h_0^2x_{124,8}]$, when multiplied by $\lambda^3 \eta$, belongs to $\AF \ge 15$, given that $[h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$ has $\AF=14$.
2256 
2257     Since $[h_0^2x_{124,8}]$ has $\AF=10$, the indeterminacy is generated by cycles in $\AF \ge 11$ of stem 124. We observe that classical cycles in $\AF=11, 12$ of stem 124 are all killed by Adams $d_2$ or $d_3$-differentials, and cycles in $\AF=13$ are all annihilated by $h_1$ in Ext. Therefore, we conclude that the indeterminacy, when multiplied by $\lambda^3 \eta$, belongs to $\AF \ge 15$.
2258 \end{proof}
2259 
2260 \begin{remark}
2261     From Fact~\ref{fact:theta5sqAF}(2) and the rigidity Theorems~\ref{thm:rigid} and \ref{thm:17e90ac0} for the synthetic Adams spectral sequence for $S^{0,0}$, the right side of the equation in statement $(5')$ is nonzero if and only if statement $(3)$ is true.
2262 \end{remark}

Then by Theorem~\ref{thm:bjmbx}(2) we have that $h_6^2$ is a permanent cycle in the classical Adams spectral sequence.\\

2296 By Lemmas~\ref{lem:equistate4} and \ref{lem:equistate5}, we will freely interchange statements $(4)$ and $(4')$, as well as $(5)$ and $(5')$, depending on the context.\\
2297 
2298 Now we prove Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq}.
2299 
2300 \begin{proof}[Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq}]
2301     From the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:bjmbx} \cite{BurklundXu} we know that
2302     $$\delta_1(h_6^2) = \lambda \eta \theta_5^2,$$
2303     where $\delta_1$ is the map $S^{0,0}/\lambda \rightarrow S^{1,-1}$. Suppose that $\eta \theta_5^2$ is detected by $\lambda^{n-5} T_n$ in $\AF = n$ of the synthetic Adams spectral sequence for some $T_n \in \Ext_A^{n, 125+n}$. This implies a differential in the $\delta_1$-ESS:
2304     $$d_{n-2}^{\delta_1} (h_6^2) = \lambda^{n-2} T_n.$$
2305     By Theorems~\ref{thm:rigid}, \ref{thm:17e90ac0} and Corollary~\ref{cor:2a636737}, this is equivalent to a 
2306     synthetic Adams differential
2307     $$d_{n-2} (h_6^2) = \lambda^{n-3} T_n$$
2308     and a classical Adams differential
2309     $$d_{n-2} (h_6^2) = T_n.$$
2310     This differential is nonzero if and only if $T_n$ is nonzero on the classical $E_{n-2}$-page, i.e., not the image of a differential $d_{\le n-3}$. In particular, when $\lambda \eta \theta_5^2=0$, we conclude that $h_6^2$ is a permanent cycle.
2311 
2312     We first show that statements $(3)$, $(4')$ and $(5')$ together imply statement $(2)$.
2313 
2314     By statements $(4')$ and $(5')$, we have that for any $\theta_5$,
2315     $$\lambda \eta \theta_5^2 = \lambda \eta \cdot \lambda^6 [h_0^2x_{124,8}] = \lambda^4 \cdot \lambda^3 \eta [h_0^2x_{124,8}] = \lambda^{10} [h_1h_4x_{109,12}].$$
2316 Recall from Fact~\ref{fact:theta5sqAF}(2) that $h_1h_4x_{109,12}$ is a permanent cycle, and can only be killed classically by 
2317     $$d_6(x_{126,8,4} + x_{126,8}) \ \text{or} \ d_{12}(h_6^2).$$
2318     Therefore, statement $(3)$ implies that the expression $\lambda^{10} [h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$ is nonzero in synthetic homotopy, leading to a nonzero classical $d_{12}$-differential:
2319     $$d_{12}(h_6^2) = h_1h_4x_{109,12}.$$
2320     
2321 We will complete the proof of
2322     Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} by showing that if one the statements $(3)$, $(4)$ or $(5)$ is false, then statement $(2)$ is also false, and in this case, 
2323     $$\eta \theta_5^2 = 0 \in \pi_{125,125+5} S^{0,0},$$ 
2324     making statement $(1)$ true. This conclusion is reached by estimating the Adams filtration of $\theta_5^2$ and subsequently that of the expression $\eta \theta_5^2$. 
2325 
2326 
2327 
2328 We begin by estimating the Adams filtration of $\theta_5^2$ in   $\pi_{124,124+4} S^{0,0}$. First, we observe that this group  $\pi_{124,124+4} S^{0,0}$ does not contain any $\lambda$-torsion classes. 
2329 This is because, in the 125-stem, the group (from Table~\ref{Table:S125.19} in the Appendix) 
2330 $$\Ext_A^{i,125+i} = 0 \ \text{for} \ i\leq 4,$$ 
2331 and thus, by the rigidity Theorems~\ref{thm:rigid} and \ref{thm:17e90ac0} for the synthetic Adams spectral sequence for $S^{0,0}$, in the 124-stem, $\lambda$-torsion classes can only appear in $\pi_{124,124+j} S^{0,0}$ for $j \geq 7$.
2332 
2333 Additionally, by analyzing classical Adams differentials, the Adams filtration of $\theta_5^2$ is at least 10. In the case where it is of Adams filtration 10, it is detected by the element $h_0^2 x_{124,8}$, which, according to Fact~\ref{fact:theta5sqAF}(3), is a permanent cycle and cannot be killed. 
2334 
2335 Upon further inspection of the differentials associated with elements in stem 124 and filtration between 10 and 13, we are left with three possibilities:
2336 \begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
2337     \item $\theta_5^2 = \lambda^6 \cdot [h_0^2 x_{124,8}]$, which is statement (4), or
2338     \item $\theta_5^2 = \lambda^9 \cdot [e_0 \Delta h_6 g]$, where $e_0 \Delta h_6 g$ is permanent cycle in $\AF=13$, or
2339     \item $\theta_5^2$ is a $\lambda^{10}$-multiple.
2340 \end{enumerate}
2341 
2342 Since in Ext, we have $h_1 \cdot e_0 \Delta h_6 g = 0$, we deduce that in either possibilities (b) or (c), $\eta \theta_5^2$ is a $\lambda^{10}$-multiple. In other words, $\eta \theta_5^2$ has $\AF \ge 15$. In the group $\pi_{125,125+5} S^{0,0}$, the only class that is a $\lambda^{10}$-multiple is actually $\lambda$-free: By Fact~\ref{fact:theta5sqAF}(4), it is $\lambda^{20} g^4 \Delta h_1 g$ in $\AF=25$ and is detected by tmf (see \cite{tmf} for the Hurewicz image of tmf). Since $\theta_5$ maps to zero in tmf, we have that $\eta \theta_5^2$ maps to zero in tmf and therefore must be zero in this case. 
2343 
2344 This shows that if statement (4) is false, then $\eta \theta_5^2 = 0$, and therefore $h_6^2$ is a permanent cycle.
2345 
2346 Hence, we focus on the remaining possibility (a), assuming statement (4) holds:
2347 $$\theta_5^2 = \lambda^6 \cdot [h_0^2 x_{124,8}].$$
2348 
2349 By the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:equistate5}, the only nonzero possibility for $\lambda^3 \eta [h_0^2x_{124,8}]$ is $\lambda^6 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$. Therefore, if statement (5) is false, then using the same tmf detection argument, we conclude that $\eta \theta_5^2 = 0$, and consequently, $h_6^2$ is a permanent cycle.
2350 
2351 Finally, if statement (3) is false, an inspection reveals the only alternative classical differential:
2352 $$d_6(x_{126,8,4} + x_{126,8}) = h_1h_4x_{109,12}.$$
2353 This, combined with the tmf detection argument, would again imply $\eta \theta_5^2 = 0$, and consequently, $h_6^2$ is a permanent cycle. This completes the proof.
2354 \end{proof}

2357 
2358 %Next, we focus on the element $h_1h_4x_{109,12}$ in stem 125, Adams filtration 14. It is known that this is a permanent cycle, and there are three possibilities of which element could kill it (classically).

Inspection of differentials associated to elements in stem 125 and Adams filtration 14 and above tells us possibility (a) implies that $\lambda^5 \cdot y = 0$, so it won't be relevant to $\eta \theta_5^2$.

2372 Before we prove Proposition~\ref{prop:state5false}, we first state and prove a few lemmas.
2373 
2374 For Lemma~\ref{lem:x_123_9}, we draw attention to the following element in Ext:
2375 $$x_{123,9}+h_0x_{123,8} \in \Ext_A^{9, 123+9}.$$
2376 From Tables~\ref{Table:S123}, \ref{Table:S125.19} in the Appendix, we have
2377 \begin{fact} \label{fact:x_123_9}\label{fact:x1239}
2378     \begin{enumerate}
2379 
2380    \item  $x_{123,9}+h_0x_{123,8}$ survives to the Adams $E_{12}$-page, and is not killed by any classical differential.
2381       % \item In Ext, $h_1 \cdot (x_{123,9}+h_0x_{123,8}) = h_1 x_{123,9}$.
2382     \item We have a classical nonzero differential 
2383     $$d_2 (x_{125,8}) = h_1 (x_{123,9}+h_0x_{123,8}) + h_0^2x_{124,8}.$$
2384    \end{enumerate}
2385 \end{fact}
2386 
2387 \begin{lemma} \label{lem:x_123_9}\label{lem:x1239}
2388     There exists a homotopy class 
2389     $$\alpha_1 = [x_{123,9}+h_0x_{123,8}] \in \pi_{123,123+9} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$$
2390     with the following properties:
2391     \begin{enumerate}
2392     \item For any homotopy class $[h_0^2 x_{124,8}]$, there exist homotopy classes 
2393     $$\alpha_2 \in \pi_{124,124+13} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9, \ \alpha_3 \in \pi_{125,125+15} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9,$$ 
2394     such that 
2395     \begin{align*}
2396         \lambda^3 \eta \cdot \alpha_1 & = \lambda^3 [h_0^2 x_{124,8}] + 
2397 \lambda^6 \alpha_2 && \in \pi_{124,124+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9,\\
2398 \eta \cdot \alpha_2 & = \lambda \cdot \alpha_3 && \in \pi_{125,125+14} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9,
2399     \end{align*}
2400 \item \leavevmode\vspace*{-\dimexpr\abovedisplayskip + \baselineskip} 
2401 $$\lambda^3 \cdot \alpha_1 \cdot [h_0] = 0 \in \pi_{123,123+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9.$$
2402     \end{enumerate}
2403 \end{lemma}
2404 
2405 \begin{proof}
2406 From Fact~\ref{fact:x_123_9}(1), the element $x_{123,9}+h_0x_{123,8}$ survives to the Adams $E_{12}$-page, and is not killed by any classical differential.
2407 
2408 Let $\alpha_1 = [x_{123,9}+h_0x_{123,8}] \in \pi_{123,123+9} S^{0,0}/\lambda^{11}$ denote any homotopy class detected by $x_{123,9}+h_0x_{123,8}$. For simplicity, we also use  $\alpha_1$ to denote its images in $\pi_{123,123+9} S^{0,0}/\lambda^r$ for $1 \leq r \leq 10$, under the following sequence of maps:
2409 $$
2410 \xymatrix{
2411 S^{0,0}/\lambda^{11} \ar[r] & S^{0,0}/\lambda^{10} \ar[r] & \cdots \ar[r] & S^{0,0}/\lambda \\
2412 \alpha_1 = [x_{123,9}+h_0x_{123,8}] \ar@{|->}[r] & \alpha_1 \ar@{|->}[r] & \cdots \ar@{|->}[r] & x_{123,9}+h_0x_{123,8}
2413 }$$
2414 
2415 From the nonzero $d_2$-differential in Fact~\ref{fact:x_123_9},  we have 
2416 $$h_1 \cdot (x_{123,9}+h_0x_{123,8}) = h_0^2x_{124,8},$$
2417 on the classical $E_3$-page. This implies synthetically, for $2 \leq r \leq 11$,
2418 $$\lambda \eta \cdot \alpha_1 + \lambda [h_0^2 x_{124,8}] \in \pi_{124,124+9} S^{0,0}/\lambda^r$$
2419 lies in $\AF \ge 11$ for any $[h_0^2 x_{124,8}]$.
2420 
2421 %We claim that synthetically 
2422 %$$\lambda^3 \eta \cdot x = \lambda^3 [h_0^2 x_{124,8}] + 
2423 %\lambda^6 w \ \ \text{in} \ \ \pi_{124,124+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9,$$
2424 %where $w \in \pi_{124,124+13} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$  with the property that $\eta w$ is $\lambda$-divisible. 
2425 
2426 By inspection, as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:equistate5}, $$\lambda^3 \eta \cdot \alpha_1 + \lambda^3 [h_0^2 x_{124,8}] \in \pi_{124,124+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$$
2427 has $\AF \ge 13$. The only possibility for it to have $\AF=13$ is that it is detected by the element $\lambda^6 e_0 \Delta h_6g$. (Note that the class $[\lambda^6 h_4 x_{109,12}]$ is irrelevant due to the nonzero differential $d_3(\lambda^6 h_4 x_{109,12}) = \lambda^8 h_1x_{122,15,2}$.) Since in Ext we have 
2428 $$h_1 \cdot e_0 \Delta h_6g = 0,$$
2429 we may choose $\alpha_2 \in \pi_{124,124+13} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$ to be any class detected by $\lambda^6 e_0 \Delta h_6g$, with the property that $\eta \alpha_2$ is $\lambda$-divisible. Thus, there exists an $\alpha_3$ such that $\eta \alpha_2 = \lambda  \alpha_3$.
2430 
2431 This proves the required property $(1)$.
2432 
2433 For the relation in property $(2)$, we will first prove it in $\pi_{123,123+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^{11}$ and then map it to $\pi_{123,123+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$.
2434 
2435 By Proposition~\ref{prop:59f111f} for the synthetic Adams spectral sequence for $S^{0,0}/\lambda^{11}$, the expression 
2436 $$\lambda^3 \cdot \alpha_1 \cdot [h_0] \in  \pi_{123,123+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^{11}$$
2437 has $\AF \ge 17$. %(one can actually show that it has Adams filtration at least 17 but we only need it to be at least 16). 
2438 In particular, the values $$\lambda^4(x_{123,11,2}+x_{123,11}+h_0h_6B_4) \ \text{in} \ \AF=11,$$ 
2439 $$\lambda^8 h_0^2x_{123,13,2} \ \text{in} \ \AF=15$$
2440 can be ruled out due to the nonzero Adams differentials
2441 $$d_7( \lambda^4 \cdot (x_{123,11,2}+x_{123,11}+h_0h_6B_4) \ ) = \lambda^{10} h_1x_{121,17},$$
2442 $$d_3 (\lambda^8 \cdot h_0^2x_{123,13,2}) = \lambda^{10} h_0^2 x_{122,16},$$
2443 in the synthetic Adams spectral sequence for $S^{0,0}/\lambda^{11}$, which are zero in the spectral sequence for $S^{0,0}/\lambda^{9}$.
2444 
2445 Therefore, the expression $\lambda^3 \cdot \alpha_1 \cdot [h_0]$ is $\lambda^{10}$-divisible in $\pi_{123,123+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^{11}$. Mapping it further to $\pi_{123,123+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$, we conclude that it is zero. 
2446 \end{proof}

2449 %we also need to exclude $\lambda^4[x_{123,11,2}+x_{123,11}+h_0h_6[B_4]]$, but it is impossible that $\lambda [h_0] x = \lambda^2[x_{123,11,2}+x_{123,11}+h_0h_6[B_4]]$ in $S^{0,0}/\lambda^{9}$

2452 %\underline{Step 2-2.} \ Now we return to our main proof.

2463 For Lemma~\ref{lem:toda2ext}, we draw attention to the following element in Ext:
2464 $$h_0^2x_{125,9,2} \in \Ext_A^{11, 125+11}.$$
2465 From Table~\ref{Table:S125.19} in the Appendix, we have
2466 \begin{fact} \label{fact:h_0_sq_mul_x_125_9_2}\label{fact:h02x1259}
2467     The element $h_0^2x_{125,9,2}$ survives to the Adams $E_5$-page, and is not killed by any classical differential.
2468 \end{fact}
2469 
2470 \begin{lemma} \label{lem:toda2ext}
2471     Assuming that both statements $(3)$ and $(5')$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} are true, the synthetic Toda bracket
2472 $$\langle \lambda^3 \alpha_1, [h_0], \eta \rangle \subset \pi_{125,125+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$$
2473     does not contain zero, and is detected by $\lambda^4 h_0^2x_{125,9,2}$. Here $\alpha_1 = [x_{123,9}+h_0x_{123,8}]$ refers to the homotopy class described in Lemma~\ref{lem:x_123_9}.
2474 \end{lemma}
2475 
2476 Note that the synthetic Toda bracket in Lemma~\ref{lem:toda2ext} is well defined, as the homotopy class $\alpha_1$ in Lemma~\ref{lem:x_123_9} satisfies the relation $\lambda^3 \alpha_1 \cdot [h_0] = 0$.
2477 
2478 \begin{remark} \label{rem:h_0_sq_mul_x_125_9_2}\label{rem:h02x1259}
2479     According to Fact~\ref{fact:h_0_sq_mul_x_125_9_2}, it is not yet known whether the element $h_0^2x_{125,9,2}$ supports a nonzero $d_5$-differential. Assuming that both statements $(3)$ and $(5')$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} are true, Lemma~\ref{lem:toda2ext} specifically implies that $\lambda^4 h_0^2x_{125,9,2}$ detects a nonzero homotopy class in $\pi_{125,125+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$. Therefore, under these assumptions, we would have $d_5(h_0^2x_{125,9,2}) = 0$. 
2480 \end{remark}
2481 
2482 \begin{proof}[Proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:toda2ext}]
2483     We assume both statements $(3)$ and $(5')$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} are true. From statement $(5')$, we have 
2484     $$\lambda^3 \eta [h_0^2x_{124,8}] = \lambda^6 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}] \in \pi_{125,125+8} S^{0,0}.$$
2485 Mapping this relation to $S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$, and applying the following relations from Lemma~\ref{lem:x_123_9}
2486 \begin{align*}
2487         \lambda^3 \eta \cdot \alpha_1 & = \lambda^3 [h_0^2 x_{124,8}] + 
2488 \lambda^6 \alpha_2 && \in \pi_{124,124+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9,\\
2489 \eta \cdot \alpha_2 & = \lambda \cdot \alpha_3 && \in \pi_{125,125+14} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9,
2490     \end{align*}
2491 we have
2492 \begin{align*}
2493     \eta \cdot \lambda^3 \eta \alpha_1 & = \eta \cdot \lambda^3 [h_0^2 x_{124,8}] + \eta \cdot \lambda^6 \alpha_2\\
2494    & = \lambda^6 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}] + \lambda^7 \alpha_3 \in \pi_{125,125+8} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9,
2495  \end{align*}
2496  which, from statement $(3)$ and Remark~\ref{rem:h_0_sq_mul_x_125_9_2}, is nonzero and detected by $\lambda^6 h_1h_4x_{109,12}$ in $\AF=14$.
2497  
2498  On the other hand, since $\eta^2 = \langle [h_0], \eta, [h_0] \rangle$, we have
2499  $$\eta \cdot \lambda^3 \eta \alpha_1 = \lambda^3 \alpha_1 \cdot \langle [h_0], \eta, [h_0] \rangle = \langle \lambda^3 \alpha_1, [h_0], \eta \rangle \cdot [h_0].$$
2500  Therefore, the synthetic Toda bracket
2501 $\langle \lambda^3 \alpha_1, [h_0], \eta \rangle$
2502     does not contain zero, and its $[h_0]$-multiple is detected by $\lambda^6 h_1h_4x_{109,12}$ in $\AF=14$. Since $h_1h_4x_{109,12}$ is not $h_0$-divisible in Ext, the synthetic Toda bracket is detected by an element in $\AF \le 12$.
2503     
2504 
2505 This synthetic Toda bracket $\langle \lambda^3 \alpha_1, [h_0], \eta \rangle$ lies in $\pi_{125,125+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$, whose $\AF \le 12$ part is generated by
2506 \begin{align*}
2507 [h_0^2 x_{125,5}] \ & \text{in} \ \AF=7,\\ 
2508 \lambda^2 [h_6(\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2)] \ & \text{in} \ \AF=9,\\ 
2509 [\lambda^4 h_0^2 x_{125,9,2}] \ & \text{in} \ \AF=11.
2510 \end{align*}
2511 %\ [\lambda^6 h_3 x_{118,12}], \ \lambda^7 y, \ [\lambda^7x_{125,14}].$$
2512 
2513 
2514 %also need to check the output data to have $d_3(x_{126,6})$
2515 
2516 %Note that, %due to the nonzero differential 
2517 %$$d_3(h_2 \cdot x_{126,6}) = \lambda^2 h_2 \cdot h_5 x_{94,8},$$
2518 %we deduce that 
2519 From Table~\ref{Table:S126.10} in the Appendix, we have
2520 $$0 \neq d_3(x_{126,6}) = \lambda^2 h_5 x_{94,8} + \ \text{possibly} \ \lambda^2 h_6(\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2).$$ 
2521 In both scenarios, $\lambda^2 [h_6(\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2)]$ remains and is in $\AF=9$.
2522 
2523 For the rest of the proof, we only need to rule out the cases $[h_0^2 x_{125,5}]$ in $\AF=7$ and $\lambda^2 [h_6(\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2)]$ in $\AF=9$. 
2524 
2525 For $[h_0^2 x_{125,5}]$ in $\AF=7$, due to the nonzero $d_3$-differential
2526 $$d_3(x_{126,4}) = \lambda^2 h_0^2 x_{125,5},$$
2527 it can be chosen to be annihilated by $\lambda^2$. 
2528 
2529 However, from statement $(3)$ and Remark~\ref{rem:h_0_sq_mul_x_125_9_2}, $\lambda^8 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$ remains nonzero in the homotopy of $S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$. Therefore, the synthetic Toda bracket is not annihilated by $\lambda^2$. As discussed earlier, its $[h_0]$-multiple is detected by $\lambda^6 h_1h_4x_{109,12}$, and thus, this case of $[h_0^2 x_{125,5}]$ can be ruled out.
2530 
2531 
2532 
2533 For $\lambda^2 [h_6(\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2)]$ in $\AF=9$, we first consider a classical Toda bracket in stem 125:
2534 $$\langle \theta_5, 2, [\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2] \rangle.$$
2535 
2536 From \cite{Xu, IWX}, $\pi_{62} \cong \mathbb{Z}/2 \oplus \mathbb{Z}/2 \oplus \mathbb{Z}/2 \oplus \mathbb{Z}/2$, so in particular both $\theta_5$ and $[\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2]$ have order 2, and this classical Toda bracket is well defined.
2537 
2538 From classical $d_2$-differentials:
2539 $$d_2(h_6) = h_0h_5^2, \ d_2(h_0^6h_6)= h_0(\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2),$$
2540 we obtain the following Massey product on the $E_3$-page
2541 $$h_6(\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2) = \langle h_5^2, h_0, \Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2 \rangle,$$
2542 and we check that it has zero indeterminacy. Further analysis shows that there are no crossing differentials, as per the criteria of Moss's theorem \cite[Theorem~1.2]{Moss} (noting that crossing differentials in Moss's theorem have a different meaning from our definition). Therefore, we have a classical Toda bracket 
2543 $$[h_6(\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2)] \in \langle \theta_5, 2, [\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2] \rangle.$$
2544 
2545 Synthetically, by inspection, we also have $2\theta_5=0$ and $2 [\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2] = 0$. It follows that there is a corresponding synthetic Toda bracket
2546 $$[h_6(\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2)] \in \langle \theta_5, 2, [\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2] \rangle.$$
2547 Multiplying by $\lambda^2 [h_0]$, we get:
2548 \begin{align*}
2549     \lambda^2 [h_0] \cdot [h_6(\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2)]& = \lambda^2 [h_0] \cdot \langle \theta_5, 2, [\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2] \rangle \\
2550     & = \lambda \cdot \langle 2, \theta_5, 2 \rangle [\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2]\\
2551     & = \lambda^3 \eta \theta_5 [\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2].
2552 \end{align*}
2553 Note that all expressions in the above equation have zero indeterminacy. 
2554 
2555 If the synthetic Toda bracket $\langle \lambda^3 \alpha_1, [h_0], \eta \rangle$ were $\lambda^2 [h_6(\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2)]$, mapping the above equation to $S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$, we would then have a nonzero equation in $\pi_{125,125+8} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$.
2556 \begin{align*}
2557     \lambda^3 \eta [h_0^2 x_{124,8}] = \lambda^6 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}] & = \langle \lambda^3 \alpha_1, [h_0], \eta \rangle [h_0] + \lambda^6 \eta \alpha_2 \\ & = \lambda^2 [h_6(\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2)] [h_0] + \lambda^6 \eta \alpha_2\\ & = \lambda^3 \eta \theta_5 [\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2] + \lambda^6 \eta \alpha_2.
2558 \end{align*}
2559 For this equation to be nonzero, we must have a nonzero equation
2560 $$\lambda^3 [h_0^2 x_{124,8}] = \lambda^3 \theta_5 [\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2] + \lambda^6 \alpha_2 \ \ \text{in} \ \ \pi_{124,124+7} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9.$$
2561 However, in Ext, we have 
2562 $$h_5^2 (\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2) = 0 \neq h_0^2 x_{124,8} \in \Ext_A^{10,124+10},$$
2563 so this equation is not possible.
2564 
2565 
2566 We have ruled out the possibility that the synthetic Toda bracket $\langle \lambda^3 \alpha_1, [h_0], \eta \rangle$ is detected by $\lambda^2 [h_6(\Delta e_1 +C_0+h_0^6h_5^2)]$ or $[h_0^2 x_{125,5}]$. Therefore, we conclude that it must be detected by $[\lambda^4 h_0^2 x_{125,9,2}]$.
2567 \end{proof}
2568 
2569 From the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:toda2ext}, we have the following $[h_0]$-extension.
2570 
2571 \begin{corollary} \label{cor:2ext125}
2572     Assuming that both statements $(3)$ and $(5')$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} are true, we have a relation: For any homotopy class $[\lambda^4 h_0^2x_{125,9,2}]$,
2573     $$[\lambda^4 h_0^2x_{125,9,2}] \cdot [h_0] = \lambda^6 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}] \neq 0 \in \pi_{125,125+8} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9,$$
2574     for some $[h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$.
2575 \end{corollary}
2576 
2577 \begin{proof}
2578     From the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:toda2ext}, there exists a homotopy class $[\lambda^4 h_0^2x_{125,9,2}]$ contained in the synthetic Toda bracket $ \langle \lambda^3 \alpha_1, [h_0], \eta \rangle$, and we have
2579     $$[\lambda^4 h_0^2x_{125,9,2}] \cdot [h_0] = \langle \lambda^3 \alpha_1, [h_0], \eta \rangle \cdot [h_0] = \lambda^6 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}] + \lambda^7 \alpha_3.$$
2580     Since $\lambda^7 \alpha_3$ is in a strictly higher filtration than $\lambda^6 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$, we may choose another class $[h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$ such that the right-hand side of the above equation is simply $\lambda^6 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$. From the discussion of this synthetic Toda bracket in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:toda2ext}, we know that the difference of any two classes detected by $\lambda^4 h_0^2x_{125,9,2}$, when multiplied by $[h_0]$, is not detected by $\lambda^6 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$. Therefore, the corollary holds for any choice of $[\lambda^4 h_0^2x_{125,9,2}]$.
2581 \end{proof}
