2608 We have one more Lemma~\ref{lem:nuext125} before we prove Proposition~\ref{prop:state5false}.
2609 
2610 We draw attention to the following element in Ext:
2611 $$h_1x_{121,7} \in \Ext_A^{8,122+8}.$$
2612 From Table~\ref{Table:S122} in the Appendix, we have
2613 \begin{fact} \label{fact:h_1_mul_x_121_7}\label{fact:h1x1217}
2614     The element $h_1x_{121,7}$ survives to the Adams $E_6$-page, and is not killed by any classical differential.
2615 \end{fact}
2616 
2617 \begin{lemma} \label{lem:nuext125}
2618     There exists a homotopy class $[\lambda^4 h_1 x_{121,7}] \in \pi_{122,122+4}S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$, such that 
2619     $$[\lambda^4 h_1 x_{121,7}] \cdot [h_2] = \lambda [\lambda^5 h_0^2 x_{125,9,2}] \in \pi_{125,125+5} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9.$$
2620 \end{lemma}
2621 
2622 \begin{proof}
2623     From Fact~\ref{fact:h_1_mul_x_121_7}, we know that the element $h_1x_{121,7}$ may only support a nonzero $d_r$-differential for $r \ge 6$. By Proposition~\ref{prop:6de7d130}, $\lambda^4 h_1 x_{121,7}$ detects nonzero homotopy classes in $\pi_{122,122+4}S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$, and hence the required existence of such a homotopy class.
2624 
2625     For the desired relation, we follow Example~\ref{exam:Mahowald} and apply the Generalized Mahowald Trick Theorem~\ref{thm:158d451a}. Consider the distinguished triangle
2626         $$\xymatrix{S^3 \ar[r]^{\nu} & S^0 \ar[r]^-{i} & S^0/\nu \ar[r]^{q} & S^4.}$$
2627 The short exact sequence on $\HF$-homology
2628 $$\xymatrix{0 \ar[r] & {\HF}_*S^0 \ar[r]^-{i_*} & {\HF}_*S^0/\nu \ar[r]^-{q_*} & {\HF}_*S^4 \ar[r] & 0,}$$
2629 induces a long exact sequence on Ext-groups
2630 $$\xymatrix{\cdots \ar[r]^-{\cdot h_2} & {\Ext}_A^{*,*}(S^0) \ar[r]^-{i_*} & {\Ext}_A^{*,*}(S^0/\nu) \ar[r]^-{q_*} & {\Ext}_A^{*,*}(S^4) \ar[r]^-{\cdot h_2} & \cdots}.$$
2631 Also recall for notations, if an element $x$ in ${\Ext}_A^{*,*}(S^0/\nu)$ satisfies 
2632 $$q_*(x) = a \neq 0 \in \Ext_A^{*,*}(S^4),$$ 
2633 we denote $x$ by $a[4]$; otherwise, due to exactness, 
2634 $$x = i_*(b) \ \text{for some} \ b \in \Ext_A^{*,*}(S^0),$$
2635 and in this case, we denote $x$ by $b[0]$. 
2636 
2637 We consider
2638 \begin{align*}
2639 x & = h_1 x_{121,7} && \in \Ext_A^{8,130},\\
2640 \bar{x} & =h_1 x_{121,7} [4] + x_{126,8}[0] + x_{126,8,2}[0] && \in \Ext_A^{8,134}(S^0/\nu), \\
2641 y & =h_0^2 x_{125,9,2} && \in \Ext_A^{11,136} ,\\
2642         \bar{y} & =h_0^2 x_{125,9,2}[0] && \in \Ext_A^{11,136}(S^0/\nu). 
2643   \end{align*}      
2644 
2645 For conditions in Theorem~\ref{thm:158d451a}, we have:
2646 \begin{enumerate}
2647       \item $d_0^{q, E_2}(h_1 x_{121,7} [4] + x_{126,8}[0] + x_{126,8,2}[0]) = h_1 x_{121,7}$.
2648       \item $d_3 (h_1 x_{121,7} [4] + x_{126,8}[0] + x_{126,8,2}[0]) = h_0^2 x_{125,9,2} [0]$. This is a classical Adams $d_3$-differential for $S^0/\nu$, and is obtained from our computations.
2649       \item 
2650       \begin{enumerate}
2651           \item The differential in (1) has no crossing, as it is a $d_0$-differential, and
2652           \item Upon inspection, the differential in (2) has no crossing. 
2653       \end{enumerate}
2654       \item 
2655       $d_0^{i, E_2} (h_0^2 x_{125,9,2}) = h_0^2 x_{125,9,2}[0]$, as $h_0^2 x_{125,9,2}$ is not divisible by $h_2$ in Ext.
2656       \end{enumerate}
2657     Since all conditions of Theorem~\ref{thm:158d451a} are  satisfied, we conclude that there is an $([h_2], E_4)$-extension:
2658         $$d_3^{[h_2], E_4} (h_1 x_{121,7}) = h_0^2 x_{125,9,2}.$$
2659 In other words, we have the following relation:
2660       $$[h_1 x_{121,7}] \cdot [h_2] = \lambda^2 [h_0^2 x_{125,9,2}] \in \pi_{125,125+9} S^{0,0}/\lambda^3.$$  
2661 Using the map $\rho:S^{0,0}/\lambda^5 \rightarrow S^{0,0}/\lambda^3$ from Notation~\ref{not:deltaandrho}, we lift the above relation and obtain:
2662 $$[h_1 x_{121,7}] \cdot [h_2] = [\lambda^2 h_0^2 x_{125,9,2}] \in \pi_{125,125+9} S^{0,0}/\lambda^5.$$
2663 In fact, since $h_1 x_{121,7} \cdot h_2 = 0$ in $\AF=9$ of Ext, we might obtain a relation of the form:
2664 $$[h_1 x_{121,7}] \cdot [h_2] = [\lambda x] + [\lambda^2 h_0^2 x_{125,9,2}] \in \pi_{125,125+9} S^{0,0}/\lambda^5,$$
2665 for some element $x$ in $\AF=10$. However, upon inspection, for all $x \in  \Ext_A^{10,125+10}$, the homotopy class $[\lambda x]$ either does not exist or can be chosen to be zero.
2666 
2667 
2668 
2669 
2670 Using the map $\lambda^4: \Sigma^{0,-4}S^{0,0}/\lambda^5 \rightarrow S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$ from Notation~\ref{not:deltaandrho}, we further push the above relation and obtain the following relation:
2671 $$[\lambda^4 h_1 x_{121,7}] \cdot [h_2] = \lambda [\lambda^5 h_0^2 x_{125,9,2}] \in \pi_{125,125+5} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9.$$
2672 This completes the proof.
2673 \end{proof}

2680 
2681 Now we prove Proposition~\ref{prop:state5false}.
2682 
2683 We draw attention to the following elements in Ext:
2684 \begin{align*}
2685 h_6 M d_0 & \in \Ext_A^{11,122+11}, \\
2686 h_5x_{91,11} & \in \Ext_A^{12,122+12}.
2687 \end{align*}
2688 From Table~\ref{Table:S122} in the Appendix, we have
2689 \begin{fact} \label{fact:stem122}
2690 \begin{enumerate}
2691     \item[] 
2692     \item The element $h_6 M d_0$ survives to the Adams $E_\infty$-page.
2693     \item The element $h_5x_{91,11}$ survives to the Adams $E_\infty$-page.
2694 \end{enumerate}
2695     
2696 \end{fact}
2697 
2698 \begin{proof}[Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:state5false}]
2699     We assume that statement $(3)$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq} is true. For the sake of a contradiction, we also assume statement $(5')$ is true.
2700 
2701     From Lemma~\ref{lem:nuext125} and Corollary~\ref{cor:2ext125}, there exists a homotopy class\\ $[\lambda^4 h_1 x_{121,7}] \in \pi_{122,122+4}S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$, such that
2702 \begin{align*}
2703     [\lambda^4 h_1 x_{121,7}] \cdot [h_2] \cdot [h_0] & = \lambda [\lambda^5 h_0^2 x_{125,9,2}] \cdot [h_0] \\
2704     & = \lambda^8 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}] \neq 0 \in \pi_{125,125+6} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9,
2705 \end{align*} 
2706     for some $[h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$. Note that statement $(3)$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:possible_h_6_sq}, Fact~\ref{fact:theta5sqAF}(2), and Proposition~\ref{prop:6de7d130} imply that the expression  $\lambda^8 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$ is nonzero in the homotopy of $S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$.
2707 
2708 Since the element detecting $h_1h_4x_{109,12}$ in not an $h_2$-multiple in Ext, we must have the expression
2709 $$[\lambda^4 h_1 x_{121,7}] \cdot [h_0]$$
2710 in $\pi_{122,122+5} S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$ be nonzero, and have $\AF \le 12$. Upon inspection, the only possibilities are:
2711 $$\lambda^6 [h_6 M d_0] \ \text{in} \ \AF=11,  \ \ \ \lambda^7 [h_5x_{91,11}] \ \text{in} \ \AF=12.$$
2712 From Fact~\ref{fact:stem122} both $h_6 M d_0$ and $h_5x_{91,11}$ are nonzero permanent cycles in the classical Adams spectral sequence, so either possibility would lift to a relation in the homotopy groups of $S^{0,0}$.
2713 
2714 For the case of $\lambda^6 [h_6 M d_0]$, consider the expression
2715 $$\lambda^2 [h_6 M d_0] \cdot [h_2] \in \pi_{125, 125+10}S^{0,0}.$$
2716 Since $h_6 M d_0 \cdot h_2$ in $\AF=12$ is killed by a classical $d_2$-differential, we know that 
2717 $$\lambda [h_6 M d_0] \cdot [h_2] \in \pi_{125, 125+11}S^{0,0}$$ 
2718 is in $\AF \ge 13$. Upon inspection, the permanent cycles in $\Ext_A^{13, 125+13}$ are all killed by $d_2$ or $d_4$-differentials, and thus, $$\lambda^2 [h_6 M d_0] \cdot [h_2] \in \pi_{125, 125+10}S^{0,0}$$ 
2719 is in $\AF \ge 14$. Therefore, for the above relation in the homotopy of $S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$ to hold, we must have 
2720 $$\lambda^2 [h_6 M d_0] \cdot [h_2] = \lambda^4 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}] \in \pi_{125, 125+10} S^{0,0}.$$
2721 
2722 For the case of $\lambda^7 [h_5x_{91,11}]$, consider the expression
2723 $$\lambda [h_5x_{91,11}] \cdot [h_2] \in \pi_{125, 125+12}S^{0,0}.$$
2724 Since $h_5x_{91,11} \cdot h_2$ in $\AF=13$ is killed by a classical $d_2$-differential, we know that
2725 $$\lambda [h_5x_{91,11}] \cdot [h_2] \in \pi_{125, 125+12}S^{0,0}$$
2726 is in $\AF \ge 14$.
2727 Therefore, for the above relation in the homotopy of $S^{0,0}/\lambda^9$ to hold, we must have
2728 $$\lambda [h_5x_{91,11}] \cdot [h_2] = \lambda^2 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}] \in \pi_{125, 125+12}S^{0,0}.$$
2729 
2730 In both cases, $\lambda^4 [h_1h_4x_{109,12}]$ is a $\lambda [h_2]$-multiple in the homotopy of $S^{0,0}$. Since the classical $\nu \in \pi_3$ has $\AF=1$, we have 
2731 $$S^{0,0}/(\lambda [h_2]) \simeq \nu (S^0/\nu).$$
2732 By the rigidity Theorem~\ref{thm:rigid} of the synthetic Adams spectral sequence for $S^{0,0}/(\lambda [h_2])$, we know that the element $\lambda^4 h_1h_4x_{109,12}[0]$ must be killed by a synthetic Adams differential, which corresponds to to a statement that in the classical Adams spectral sequence of $S^0/\nu$, the element $h_1h_4x_{109,12}[0]$ must be killed by a $d_r$-differential for $r \leq 5$.
2733 
2734 
2735

2774 
2775 %\end{center}
2776 
2777 However, from Table~\ref{Table:Cnu126} (obtained from \cite{LWXMachine} \cite{LWXZenodo}, and can be visualized from \cite{LinPlot}), in the classical Adams spectral sequence of $S^0/\nu$, the element  $h_1h_4x_{109,12}[0]$ is not killed by any $d_r$ for $r \leq 5$ (from the range of $9 \le s \le 14$ in stem 126). Therefore, we arrive at a contradiction.
2778 \end{proof}

2782 
2783 \section{Appendix: The classical Adams spectral sequence in the range $122 \le t-s \le 127, s \le 25$} \label{sec:App}
2784 
2785 We provide a brief overview of Lin's computer program for computing Adams differentials and extensions. The program's functionality for propagating differentials and extensions relies on the following data:
2786 \begin{itemize}
2787     \item The Adams $E_2$-pages of a collection of CW spectra.
2788     \item Maps between these $E_2$-pages.
2789     \item Adams $d_2$-differentials for certain CW spectra.
2790     \item There are three manually added differentials: $d_5 h_0^{24}h_6=h_0^2P^6d_0$ and\\
2791  $d_6 h_0^{55}h_7=h_0^2x_{126,60}$ in the Adams spectral sequence of $S^0$ (from the image of $J$), and $d_3 v_2^{16}=\beta^5g$ in the Adams spectral sequence of $\mathit{tmf}$, derived from power operations (Bruner--Rognes \cite{BR21}).
2792 \end{itemize}
2793 
2794 Detailed descriptions of these data are available in \cite{LWXZenodo}. Lin’s program extends these results by computing additional Adams differentials using tools such as the Leibniz rule, naturality, the Generalized Leibniz Rule, and the Generalized Mahowald Trick. All computed differentials and extensions are accessible via interactive plots \cite{LinPlot}.
2795 
2796 Moreover, the proofs provided in \cite{LWXZenodo} offer more information than the interactive plots. These proofs include numerous disproofs of potential differentials, even for cases where the differentials remain unresolved. For example, consider 
2797 $$x_{126,21}\in E_2^{21,126+21}(S^0).$$
2798 The spectral sequence plot for $S^0$ shows that $x_{126,21}$ survives to the $E_4$-page, but the value of $d_4(x_{126,21})$ undetermined. By analyzing the proofs in \cite{LWXZenodo}, we observe that many potential values for $d_4(x_{126,21})$ have been ruled out. Consequently, we conclude:
2799 $$d_4(x_{126,21})=x_{125,25,2}+x_{125,25}+g^4\Delta h_1g+\text{possibly }d_0^2e_0gB_4.$$
2800 
2801 Tables~\ref{Table:S122}--\ref{Table:S127.9} present results from Lin’s program for the classical Adams spectral sequence of the sphere in the range $122 \le t-s \le 127, s \le 25$.
